Fix primary data and a mixed vertex, including the closed-$Z$
vertex at a fixed positive width. With
Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}, Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} and
the compactifications of Paper~I, its mixed maps satisfy the following
integral chain homotopies.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item If $f_0,f_1$ are two sufficiently small invariant gauge
trajectory perturbations vanishing near the whole primary critical
set, with the same symplectic end datum, then
\[
 N_1\cont_G(f_0,f_1)-N_0=d_S H_G+H_Gd_{G,0}.
\]
\item If the gauge end is fixed and $J_0,J_1$ are regular ordinary
strip structures on the same ordered primary pair, then
\[
 \cont_S(J_0,J_1)N_0-N_1=d_{S,1}H_S+H_Sd_G.
\]
In the closed-$Z$ case of part~(ii), the output pair is the product
pair $(A_0,A_1)$ and the structures and continuation are split.
\end{enumerate}
Both homotopies count actual isolated universal solutions of vertical
index $-1$. The assertion concerns these fixed-primary end changes;
it is not a mixed continuation theorem for arbitrary primary paths
or arbitrary correspondence-output pairs.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
First record the finite compact-data comparison required below.
Gauge sources may vary with fixed compact dependence and output,
and section structures may vary on a compact set, keeping all far
ends, protected genus matching collars and collapse germs fixed.
Coefficients at an ordinary physical Lagrangian boundary may vary
through a compact compatible family; its totally real labels remain
fixed. Keep split structures on a product target. The mixed local
primal and weak-adjoint estimates vary continuously with these
coefficients. A compact principal-coefficient change in the section
is not called a relatively compact operator: local elliptic estimates,
a finite parameter cover and the fixed end parametrices instead
give Fredholm estimates with a compact remainder. Both full operator
rows and all stationary columns are transported.

For zero Hamiltonian the section energy is twice its symplectic
area, even for domain-dependent $J$. There is no derivative-of-$J$
term. The gauge modified-energy formula and the relative index-period
formula give a uniform energy and lower-index bound. Compactness
retains the coefficient family on the core; the usual nonnegative
four-index loss and positive indices of ordinary breaks remain.
Invariant compact gauge variations still detect the entire mixed
cokernel with this prescribed $J$, as in Paper~I, Theorem~5.6: the scalar inverse removes the
scalar component; character-zero holonomy tests, including every
nonlocal output in the detecting region, remove the gauge adjoint;
and the matching Green form followed by zero extension and unique
continuation removes the section adjoint on each component. This
uses the genus matching arc and connected gauge region of the
stated vertices.

Finite selection is performed in increasing index from a common
lower bound. Absence of lower-index cores makes the next-index
space compact before its regularity is invoked. Freeze a finite
set of detecting variations at that stage; small coefficients
preserve the earlier absence and endpoint collars. Relative
Sard transversality gives the required universal spaces. In their
operator $(v,\tau)\mapsto D_bv+\tau C_b$, the single column $C_b$
includes $(\partial_bJ_b)u_t$, target transport and gauge-source
variation. Ordinary gluing copies this column once on the retained
core. The compactified one-dimensional space has only its data
endpoints and ordinary index-one breaks. Strong convergence and
the ordinary bordered inverse give both directions of its collars.
Parameter-first orientation gives the Hom identity
\eqref{eq:family-boundary}. This proves the finite comparison even
when the two data differ in compact section structure.

For part~(i), let $\beta$ rise from zero to one on $[-1,1]$ and
let $\chi$ be the mixed vertex switch, equal to one for $s\leq-3$
and zero for $s\geq-2$. On its incoming gauge end insert the
slice functional
\[
 q_T(s)=\chi(s)\bigl(f_0+\beta(s+T)(f_1-f_0)\bigr),
 \qquad T\geq5.
\]
It retains $f_0$ on the far end and $f_1$ on the long intervening
cylinder. Translate a fixed regular ordinary continuation
perturbation with the $\beta$ band and retain the $N_1$ secondary
on the mixed core. Their full dependence and output are disjoint
for large $T$. Ordinary continuation is pinned by these data;
a rigid continuation has full index zero, without a translation
quotient. The smallness neighbourhood for $f_i$ is chosen so
that no additional critical points occur: compactness of the
perturbed critical equation would otherwise give a primary
critical limit outside the neighbourhood where both functions
vanish. Their original critical points and Hessians thus agree.

The modified-curvature energy identity is
\[
 E_T=8\pi^2\kappa+2\|\eta_T(A)\|^2
                 +2\int\partial_s q_T(s)(A_s)\,ds,
 \qquad
 \partial_s q_T=\beta'(s+T)(f_1-f_0)+\chi'(s)f_1.
\]
The last integral is bounded in absolute value by
$\|f_1-f_0\|_\infty+\|f_1\|_\infty$, and the translated compact
secondary supports have uniformly bounded norm. The autonomous
$f_1$ gradient on the growing middle cylinder is part of modified
curvature, not a compact source whose $L^2$ norm is assumed bounded.
Reference configurations with the same intermediate critical lift
have cancelling cut actions. Ordinary determinant gluing gives
index additivity, so the period formula yields a uniform energy
and lower-index bound before extraction.

For part~(ii), choose a continuation structure $J_{01}(\sigma,t)$
from $J_0$ to $J_1$, with zero Hamiltonian and constant end collars.
It admits regular relative data within this restricted class.
Indeed at a nonconstant solution, the set where $u_t\ne0$ is dense
in any free transition region: vanishing on an open set would
make $u_s$ zero there and unique continuation would make the
solution constant. If a universal adjoint field is nonzero,
choose such a point where it is also nonzero. Domain-dependent
compatible variations $Y\mapsto Yu_t$ span the target fibre,
contradicting the cokernel pairing. Domain localization removes
any requirement that the whole strip image be injective. At a
constant solution the linear energy identity on the fixed tangent
Lagrangians makes the decaying kernel zero. Its index is zero by
deformation to an autonomous constant strip; transversality keeps
the asymptotic operators invertible during this index comparison.
For product outputs use independent variations on each nonconstant
factor; every constant factor has that inverse. Relative
Sard--Smale, monotonicity and the four-index bubble exclusion give
regular continuation spaces through index one, with the parameter
counted once.

On the outgoing ordinary strip of $N_0$, keep its original datum
until $s=T-1$ and then use $J_{01}(s-T,t)$. The external equation
is $J_1$ for every finite $T$. The gauge and stationary mixed
ends remain fixed. The transition adds no Hamiltonian curvature
or boundary work. Consequently the section area-index relation,
fixed gauge-source bounds and cancellation of intermediate actions
give the same length-independent energy and lower-index bounds.
In both parts the finite-$T$ operator has fixed far-end equations;
its length derivative is supported in the translated transition
band. At $T=\infty$ use separate retained-core charts, rather
than declaring that support fixed on the untranslated domain.

We verify exhaustiveness in both constructions. In part~(i) the
retained factors are the pinned gauge continuation and the $N_1$
core; in part~(ii) they are the $N_0$ core and the pinned strip
continuation. The fixed equations govern the intervening autonomous
neck. Extraction gives these two cores and ordinary trajectories
$W_j$, with
\[
 d-I_1-I_2-\sum_j\ind D_{W_j}=4n,\qquad
 E_{\rm lost}=4\pi^2 n,\qquad n\geq0.
\]
The continuation contributes its full index. All retained cores
are regular with nonnegative index; every nonconstant autonomous
trajectory has positive full index. Hence negative-index solutions
are absent for sufficiently large $T$, and at $d=0$ both cores
have index zero, all ordinary breaks are absent and $n=0$.
The common lower bound makes the negative-index conclusion
simultaneous. On the gauge continuation core the source switches
are retained; on the section continuation core no derivative-of-$J$
energy is missing.

Zero loss and the original end gaps give strong convergence on the
components. Leaving an intermediate critical neighbourhood costs
positive neck energy; inside it the gapped cylinder or strip inverse
and the small nonlinear remainder control the neck by the component
tails. The convergence therefore enters the strong pregluing charts.
Conversely each pair of invertible zero-index core operators gives
an ordinary bordered neck inverse and an exponentially small gluing
error. Contraction gives one solution. There are finitely many rigid
pairs, so one threshold works for all of them; strong convergence
and uniqueness rule out any other solution or duplicate chart.

The actual lifting quotient is retained in the gauge construction.
The connected cylinder contributes no extra central matching
class, and its arrows identify the two end lifting groups; the
fibre product over the common critical orbit gives precisely
matrix-composition multiplicity. A pure strip adds no gauge region
or lifting factor. In both cases ordinary determinant gluing
compares the product before cancelling the intermediate generator
line. For a nonautonomous continuation keep its full stabilized
operator on the compact core, its two prescribed end models, and
the autonomous inverse only on the matched tail. The bordered
exact sequence and the reference and cutoff squares therefore
apply with both far ends fixed. They do not replace the compact
continuation core by an autonomous trajectory. Relative mixed
shifting (Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}) and associativity preserve the
constant-pair normalization;
the constant strip has its canonical orientation. All maps here
are even. Thus the exact rigid counts are
\[
 F_T=N_1\cont_G(f_0,f_1)\quad\text{in (i)},\qquad
 F_T=\cont_S(J_0,J_1)N_0\quad\text{in (ii)}.
\]
No independent sign is chosen for a component or matrix entry.

Choose one such finite $T_*$. If necessary make an arbitrarily
small compact endpoint perturbation to regularize index one,
preserving the already regular rigid count by compactness and
local uniqueness. In part~(i), interpolate the inserted data to
$N_0$ by replacing $\beta$ with $b\beta$ and interpolating the
compact sources; the far input remains $f_0$. In part~(ii),
interpolate to $N_1$ through compact compatible section changes
and compact gauge sources, with far output $J_1$. Both paths
fit the finite comparison proved at the start. Its actual
vertical-index-minus-one count gives $H_G$ or $H_S$ and the
displayed identities. Finally Proposition~\ref{prop:weights}
extends these physical counts from their common small construction
weight to the full permitted intervals. Slow reference paths
are prepared before comparing their weights.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Parametric orientations}
The same reasoning applies to compact parameter families
of primary and geometric data whenever their asymptotic
intersections remain transverse. Locally choose
fixed-center charts and stabilizations. A nearby regular
solution has small coordinates in such a chart. The
line orientation pulled back from
Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} varies continuously, by
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} for families with varying data,
while the canonical orientation of an invertible
operator is continuous on the invertible locus. Their
ratio is consequently locally constant. This fact will
be used in the collapse and closed-cut comparisons.
It does not require identifying the topology of every
Sobolev quotient with the smooth quotient globally.

\section{Triangles and orientations under collapse}\label{sec:collapse}

\subsection{The triangle product}
Order three Lagrangians $L_0,L_1,L_2$ by the boundary
orientation of the triangle. Write $C_{ij}=\CF(L_i,L_j)$.
Use the end gradings induced by its chosen almost-complex gauge
triangle. Orient the positive-scale mixed triangle by the ordinary
cobordism reference above. The inverse collapse construction below,
followed by ordered cancellation of its completing vertices,
defines the pure triangle orientation. Put all generator lines
before its determinant line.
The resulting product
\[
 \mu:C_{01}\otimes_R C_{12}\longrightarrow C_{02}
\]
satisfies
\begin{equation}\label{eq:leibniz}
 d\mu(a,b)=(-1)^{|b|}\mu(da,b)+\mu(a,db).
\end{equation}
Indeed the first strip break requires its translation
line to pass the second input generator; the second
break does not. This is the boundary calculation for
the one-dimensional triangle space.

If $\theta\in C_{12}$ is a homogeneous cycle, then
$R_\theta(a)=\mu(a,\theta)$ is a closed map of degree
$|\theta|$. The product itself is even in this convention.
For a change of gauge base at any of the three vertices,
the corresponding symplectic conversion is diagonal on
the same generator lines. Applying the conversions with
\eqref{eq:tensor-maps} gives the product in the new bases.
If the three conversions have parities $t_{01},t_{12},t_{02}$,
the converted product has degree $m=t_{01}+t_{12}+t_{02}$ and satisfies
\[
d\mu(a,b)=(-1)^m\bigl((-1)^{|b|}\mu(da,b)+\mu(a,db)\bigr).
\]
Inserting a cycle $\theta$ then gives a closed map of degree
$m+|\theta|$. All subsequent changes of bases use this converted
formula, with the right tensor convention.

\subsection{The determinant comparison at the collapsed limit}
The comparison must be defined on compact families of limiting
configurations, where kernels and cokernels may occur. We first give
that construction. Stationary mixed-end coordinates are included
as finite-dimensional domain factors throughout.

\begin{lemma}[Collapse of stabilized families]\label{lem:collapse-family}
Let $\{V_b\}_{b\in B}$ be a compact smooth family of limiting
configurations with the specified autonomous end models and
separated stationary coordinates. Assume that their remainders
have a common exponential rate $\beta$ at every required derivative
order, and choose the comparison weights in common gaps strictly
below that rate. Retain Paper~I's unperturbed
collapse datum: the primary perturbation vanishes on the inserted
compression, and both the dependence and output of every other
perturbation avoid the prescribed collapse neighborhood.
Primary data on the remaining pieces may vary in a compact
regular family, and the length of the collapse arc may vary in a compact
range. For sufficiently small
$\epsilon>0$, pregluing gives a continuous isomorphism of
orientation lines
\begin{equation}\label{eq:collapse-det}
 \det D_{V_b}\longrightarrow\det L_{W_\epsilon(V_b)}.
\end{equation}
It is independent, up to positive homotopy, of the stabilization
and cutoff choices. It commutes with gauge and strip gluing and
with the lifting actions. At a regular index-zero solution both
operators are invertible and~\eqref{eq:collapse-det} carries the
canonical element to the canonical element.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Stabilization.} Choose a finite-dimensional real space $F$
and smooth compactly supported target columns
$j_b:F\to Y_b$ so that
$\widehat D_b=(D_{V_b},j_b):X_b\oplus F\to Y_b$ is onto.
Such columns exist by smoothness of the cokernel on the limit domain
(Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}(a),(b), which includes the
zero-scale limit domains) and density of
compactly supported tests; a finite cover of $B$ and parameter
cutoffs give one $F$. Write $K_b=\ker\widehat D_b$. These kernels
form a vector bundle. Choose projections $\Gamma_b:X_b\oplus F\to K_b$
whose restriction to $K_b$ is the identity. They can be formed
from compactly supported pairings, together with the stationary
coordinate projections at original ends and the coordinate tests
on $F$: a nonzero
kernel element cannot vanish in all three sets of tests. The $F$
tests also detect redundant or inactive stabilization columns.
A finite selection works over each compact parameter patch. A family of
arc lengths in a compact range is identified with a family of metrics on
one domain by diffeomorphisms supported along the arc; the constants of
Paper~I, Sections~7.1--7.7, are uniform on such families.
Any free stationary coordinate at an artificial exterior end is
also detected by compact pairings on $K_b$, after enlarging the
compact set. Choose those pairings instead of an artificial-end
limit projection: the artificial end will disappear on attachment.
Then $(\widehat D_b,\Gamma_b)$ is an isomorphism, with uniformly
bounded inverse over $B$.

Transport the columns to the preglued domain, away from the
transition annuli, and denote them by $j_{\epsilon,b}$. Patch
kernel fields by the harmonic lift on the collapsed compression
and by the usual cutoffs elsewhere. Call this map $J_{\epsilon,b}$.
Transport the compact pairings in $\Gamma_b$ in the same manner,
retaining their finite-dimensional factors in the original order.
This defines
\[
 A_{\epsilon,b}=(L_{W_\epsilon(V_b)},j_{\epsilon,b},
                         \Gamma_{\epsilon,b}):
 X_{\epsilon,b}\oplus F\longrightarrow Y_{\epsilon,b}\oplus K_b.
\]

\emph{The uniform linear estimate.} The linear estimates of
Paper~I, Section~7, apply here before assuming that $V_b$ solves
an equation. On the collapsed strip their inputs are a smooth
path of flat compression connections and a smooth section with
the prescribed boundary values. The Hodge, trace and energy
identities use these conditions and bounded derivatives of their
coefficients. The zero-order term of the section linearization
is retained. On the unchanged pieces the operator is the full
$D_{V_b}$. The compactness of $B$ bounds all these coefficients.
The end and transition estimates use only the fixed end operators,
the separated stationary coordinates, and the common gaps. Choose
the auxiliary exterior weight $\delta_*<\min(\beta,\gamma/2)$, where
$\gamma$ is the transition gap of Paper~I, Proposition~7.9, as in
Paper~I, Proposition~7.13. Paper~I's exterior
truncation gives the stationary-column error
\[
 O(\epsilon^{1/4})+
 O(\epsilon^{(\beta-\delta_*)/4}),
\]
uniformly over $B$. Its exponentially convergent coefficient
estimates and their weak-adjoint counterparts therefore apply to
the original configurations. Exact constant tails are not required.
Put $\mathsf A=1+|\log\epsilon|$. First work on the auxiliary
exterior of Paper~I, Section~7.7,
with its free stationary limits and fixed positive end weight.
On this exterior the localized estimate holds with a compact
remainder on the unchanged pieces and the limiting section.
It also holds for the adjoint. If the augmented exterior estimate
failed, normalized fields with image tending to zero would have
a nonzero limiting field in $\ker(D_{V_b},j_b,\Gamma_b)$ after
passing to a subsequence of $b$. The compression Hodge estimate
eliminates the fast complementary component, and harmonic matching
identifies the remaining component with the limiting section.
Compact convergence and the finite stationary and $F$ tests detect
the limiting field. The adjoint argument gives a field in
$\ker(D_{V_b},j_b,\Gamma_b)^*$. Both are impossible. Thus the
augmented exterior inverse is uniformly bounded. Its finite
columns and projections are supported off the new necks. Before
attachment change the exterior weight from the fixed gap weight
to $\delta_L\asymp\mathsf A^{-1}$, using the small-weight exterior
argument of Paper~I. The stationary part is integrated explicitly;
the normal part retains its fixed spectral gap. The finite compact
columns and tests are unaffected. This gives the same polynomial
loss at the small weight and avoids exponential weight costs for
sources whose supports move out along an overlap.

The actual domain requires an additional attachment argument.
Remove the sources in the middle
of each overlap using the stationary line inverse of Paper~I,
the transition inverse, with its small weight of order $\mathsf A^{-1}$.
This costs $O(\mathsf A)$; cutoff derivatives of order
$\mathsf A^{-1}$ leave bounded sources. Solve those sources on
the augmented exterior and the transitions, setting each
transition's stationary value equal to the corresponding free
exterior value. The two stationary values are independent.
Patch in the source-free bands. The normal components decay
exponentially across a band of length comparable to
$|\log\epsilon|$, so the patching error is bounded by
$C_B\mathsf A^N e^{-c|\log\epsilon|}$ for a fixed finite $N$.
A Neumann series gives a right inverse. Cutting a homogeneous
field in the same bands and applying the component inverses
proves injectivity. The stabilization columns and projections
occur only in the exterior equation and are unchanged by these
operations. Consequently
\[
 \|A_{\epsilon,b}^{-1}\|\le C_B\mathsf A^N.
\]
This is the source-removal proof of the attachment inverse in
Paper~I, with the exterior operator augmented by $F$ and $K_b$.
A bound independent of $\epsilon$ on the whole domain would be
false: stationary fields supported on a long new neck have norm
ratio of order $|\log\epsilon|$. The polynomial loss suffices
below. No equation for $V_b$ was used; its nonlinear residual is
not a term in the linear component estimates.

For $k\in K_b$, cutoff differentiation and the horizontal
velocity of the harmonic lift give
\[
 \|A_{\epsilon,b}J_{\epsilon,b}k-(0,k)\|
 \le C_B\epsilon^\sigma(1+|\log\epsilon|)^N\|k\|,
\]
for some $\sigma>0$ and finite $N$. These are the endpoint and
patching estimates of Paper~I, Sections~7.5--7.7, namely the kernel
estimate of Proposition~7.8 and the spatial estimate of
Proposition~7.9: the fast lift has weighted size
$O(\sqrt{\mathfrak s})$, where $\mathfrak s$ is the local compression
scale ($\mathfrak s=\epsilon/r$ on the middle compression in the
conformal representative of Paper~I, Section~7.7, and
$\mathfrak s=\epsilon e^s$ in the endpoint neck coordinate $s$). The
kernel field is stationary on the truncation region, so the lift
contributes only where $\mathfrak s\le\epsilon^{3/4}$. The stationary factors
are unchanged, and the fixed compact family has uniformly bounded
coefficients. Take $\sigma$ no larger than the positive exponents in
these endpoint errors. The truncation estimates are uniform on the
compact exponentially convergent family. Multiplication by the
polynomial inverse bound still gives an
error tending to zero. Hence
$k\mapsto A_{\epsilon,b}^{-1}(0,k)$ identifies $K_b$ with
$\ker(L_{W_\epsilon(V_b)},j_{\epsilon,b})$ and is asymptotic to
the patched kernel map. Cancel $\det F$ in the two identities
$\det D=\det\ker(D,j)\otimes(\det F)^*$ to obtain
\eqref{eq:collapse-det}.

\emph{Compatibility.} Two choices of stabilization are compared
by their direct sum. The exact kernel sequence for adding the
extra columns identifies the two cancellation maps. Cutoffs and
compact pairings can be interpolated; the same estimates on the
compact interpolation give a homotopy of the resulting maps.
Lifting actions preserve the operators and the harmonic lift,
so applying a lift to this construction gives the same determinant
map after stabilization independence.

For ordinary end gluing choose the stabilizations off both transition
regions. If $T$ is the ordinary gluing length, choose a cofinal
regime in which $e^{-cT}\mathsf A^{N'}\to0$ for each of the
finitely many polynomial losses occurring in the construction.
For example $T\ge C\log\mathsf A+T_0$ suffices with $C,T_0$
large. Correcting the common patched kernel at once, or first at
one neck and then at the other, now gives maps uniformly close
to that patched map. Their straight interpolation on the
finite-dimensional kernels is invertible. This compares the
ordered stabilization cancellations without assuming an estimate
on a full rectangle of independent parameters. Any two sufficiently
large gluing choices connect through this cofinal regime. The
ordinary gluing determinant maps are unchanged along that
connection by continuity. We obtain the asserted compatibility,
including its graded permutations.

At a regular index-zero solution the stabilization may be taken to
be zero. Then $K_b=0$, the realized operator is invertible, and the
estimate above makes the preglued operator invertible, so
\eqref{eq:collapse-det} carries the canonical element to the canonical
element; by stabilization independence the same holds for every
stabilization. This proves all the assertions.

The proof reruns Paper~I's arguments, not only its statements, for
stabilized families of nonsolutions. The modified steps are these: the
exterior inverse of Paper~I, Proposition~7.13, is proved for the
augmented operator $(D_{V_b},j_b,\Gamma_b)$; the common rate $\beta$
replaces the decay of actual solutions; the attachment inverse of
Paper~I, Proposition~7.15, is proved with the exterior operator
augmented by $F$ and $K_b$; and the kernel-patching estimate, proved in
Paper~I for the model strip (Proposition~7.8), is transplanted to the
glued domain as above.
\end{proof}

We use the lemma to \emph{define} the limiting triangle orientation.
Complete its incoming ordinary strip ends with the already oriented
one-input mixed vertices; the constant vertices at its generators
suffice for this definition. Orient $L_{W_\epsilon(V)}$ by the
positive-scale several-input construction above. That construction
first moves the metric, with the configuration fixed, along stages (A)
and (B) of the path of Paper~I, Lemma~9.12 (for the folded triangle,
the corresponding stages in the proof of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8), to a
metric with a product collar at the cut. The configuration spaces do not
depend on the metric (Appendix~\ref{app:shift-reduction}), so this is
transport along a path of data, continuous in $\epsilon$. Pull the
resulting orientation back through~\eqref{eq:collapse-det}, and cancel
the completing-vertex lines in cyclic order. A limiting triangle need not admit a shift
to a constant triangle: the triple intersection of its boundary
labels can be empty. The positive-scale shift need not preserve
the image of collapse pregluing either. Neither assertion enters
this definition.

Continuity for $0<\epsilon<\epsilon_0$ on a compact configuration
family compares sufficiently small scales. On overlaps choose a
common smaller scale. Stabilization independence and ordinary
gluing compare the auxiliary columns, completing vertices and necks.
For a symplectic strip at an incoming end, apply the one-input
comparison $C_x\#u\sim A\#C_y$ in its completing vertex, away from
the central collapse neighbourhood, and absorb $A$ into the ordinary
cobordism factor. Thus the full end-gluing diagram commutes before
the vertex lines are cancelled.

The same comparison applies with nonconstant completing vertices. If
$c_{01},c_{12}$ are mixed vertex solutions, shift each to
$A_{ij}\#C^{c'_{ij}}$ inside its vertex domain
(Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(a); Appendix~\ref{app:shifting} covers
the vertex domains). This is a compact family on a fixed limiting
domain, of the kind allowed in Lemma~\ref{lem:collapse-family}. Its
compatibility with gauge gluing at the original inputs, together with
the coherence of the positive-scale orientation under input gauge
gluing, absorbs $A_{ij}$. Hence at $c_{01}\#c_{12}\#u$ the pulled-back
orientation is $\Gamma_{c_{01}}\otimes\Gamma_{c_{12}}\otimes o(u)$, in
cyclic order. At an incoming break of the one-dimensional triangle space
the internal neck grows with the breaking parameter. Regroup
$C_x\#_T(w\#_{T'}u')$ as $(C_x\#w)\#_Tu'$ on a domain with a longer
internal neck. This neck runs along the collapsing arc: the output
strip of a completing vertex is bounded by $P_0$ and $P_1$, and in the
limit domain the only $P_1$ boundary is the collapse arc (Paper~I, proof
of Theorem~9.10). Lemma~\ref{lem:collapse-family} allows compact families
of lengths of this arc, so the pulled-back orientation does not depend on
the neck length over compact ranges. This proves coherence of the
defined triangle orientation without an independent component sign.

For a nonsolution reference family supplied only in a strong
weighted topology, use a smaller comparison weight below its
remainder rate. This does not put a slow nonsolution into a faster realization.
Actual solutions instead use their proved exponential decay in the
lemma and are not replaced by truncated solutions.

We may now specialize to a regular index-zero limiting solution $V$.
Its complete realized operator, and the operator at its preglue,
are invertible. Denote the actual nearby solution by
$\mathcal G_\epsilon(V)$.

It remains to compare the preglued operator with the
operator at the actual solution. A potential difficulty
is that the chart used for the contraction estimate
need not be the chart used to define the determinant
bundle. The second derivative of an arbitrary fixed
mixed chart need not have a uniform bound in the
collapse norm. We use the following argument instead.

\begin{lemma}[Transport around a square]\label{lem:square}
Let $D_{s,t}$ be a continuous family of Fredholm operators
on a square. Suppose the operators on three edges are
invertible. Parallel transport of orientations on the
fourth edge carries the canonical orientation at its
first endpoint to the canonical orientation at its
second endpoint.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The orientation double cover of the determinant bundle
is trivial over the square. Transport around its
boundary is therefore the identity. On each of the
three invertible edges the canonical orientation is a
continuous section, so transport is positive. The
remaining edge must have the same property at its
endpoints.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Affine centers at positive collapse scale]
\label{prop:affine-square}
Fix the collapse datum of Lemma~\ref{lem:collapse-family} and a
sufficiently small positive scale $\epsilon$. Let
$c_s=\Phi(sz_*)=(A_s,u_s)$, $0\leq s\leq1$, be the exact matching-chart
segment from the preglue $c_0=W_\epsilon(V)$ to the solution
$c_1=\mathcal G_\epsilon(V)$ supplied by Paper~I. At a common
sufficiently small original-end weight there is a continuous
Fredholm family $D_{s,t}$, $0\leq s,t\leq1$, whose $t=0$ edge is
the full contraction derivative and whose $t=1$ edge is the
physical orientation operator at $c_s$. It retains the finite
stationary coordinates, both operator rows, and their physical
weak-adjoint domains. If $V$ is regular of index zero, the other
three edges are invertible.

The Fredholm construction also holds in compact smooth families
of these small matching-chart neighbourhoods, including
nonsolutions. Its bounds may depend on the positive scale.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write $g=g_\epsilon$, $\lambda=\lambda_\epsilon$ and
$\widehat g=\lambda^{-2}g$ for the metrics and rescaling in
Paper~I, Sections~7--8. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-centers}
 C_{s,t}=(1-t)A_0+tA_s.
\end{equation}
The center need not solve the primary equation at a mixed end.
The exact chart fixes the normal connection trace, so the
covariant Neumann condition is common to all these centers.
A single normal gauge for $A_0$, applied to the whole family,
makes this trace zero without changing the affine relation.
For each fixed $\epsilon$, the changed compression region is
compact and its minimum scale is positive. Paper~I's strong
norms are then equivalent to ordinary weighted Sobolev norms,
with the stationary coordinates adjoined; these equivalence
constants need not be uniform as $\epsilon\to0$.

At an original mixed end $e$, choose the primary representatives
$B_e(p)$ in the spatial Neumann Coulomb slice of $B_e(0)$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-end-slice}
 d_{B_e(0)}^*(B_e(p)-B_e(0))=0,
 \qquad (B_e(p)-B_e(0))(n)=0.
\end{equation}
The spatial scalar inverse and implicit function theorem give
this local section of the actual primary family. Extend it into
the matching chart after subtracting its stationary value. This
ensures that the affine scalar equation has weighted target.
If an initial local primary section is used instead, its small
spatial gauge gives this normalization; extend that gauge with
a fixed longitudinal cutoff. The correction is quadratic in the
stationary parameters when the initial tangent is Coulomb. It
occurs on the fixed original-end geometry and preserves the
initial collapse operator and the quadratic estimates of Paper~I.
The chart segment is smooth in all stationary-subtracted norms.

We first compare scalar operators without assuming compact
support of $C_{s,t}-A_s$. With
$d\mu=\lambda^2dV_{\widehat g}=\lambda^{-2}dV_g$, the operator
$P_{C,A}=\lambda^2d_C^{*,g}d_A$ has weak form
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-scalar-form}
 \langle\xi,P_{C,A}\eta\rangle_\mu
 =\int\langle d_C\xi,d_A\eta\rangle_{\widehat g}\,d\mu.
\end{equation}
The two factors on the right are generally different. Let $C_P$
be the Poincare constant for $A$, and let $a$ bound the bracket
with $C-A$ in the rescaled metric. Then
\[
 \operatorname{Re}\langle\xi,P_{C,A}\xi\rangle_\mu
 \geq(1-aC_P)\|d_A\xi\|_\mu^2.
\]
If $aC_P<1$, the bounded coercive form gives a weak inverse.
Conjugating by an original-end weight $w=e^\omega$ adds form
terms controlled by $\|d\omega\|_\infty$ and its square. For a
common sufficiently small weight they are absorbed. Neumann
elliptic regularity gives the higher Sobolev inverses at this
fixed scale. The identical spatial argument makes $d_C^*d_B$
invertible when $B$ is a primary limit and $C$ is sufficiently
close to it, whether or not $C$ is primary flat.

We next specify all stationary columns. Let $h_{s,0,e}(p)$ be
the connection value of the contraction-chart column at the
limit $B_e(s)$ of $c_s$ at the end $e$, and let $C=C_{s,t,e}$ be the corresponding
limit of the center. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-columns}
 \psi_{s,t,e}(p)=(d_C^*d_{B_e(s)})^{-1}d_C^*h_{s,0,e}(p),
 \qquad h_{s,t,e}=h_{s,0,e}-d_{B_e(s)}\psi_{s,t,e}.
\end{equation}
The new value is $C$-Coulomb and has the same primary boundary
class. At $t=0$ the correction vanishes by
\eqref{eq:affine-end-slice}. Extend $\psi$ by a fixed end cutoff
to $\Xi_{s,t}(p)$ and put
\[
 H_{s,t}(p)=H_{s,0}(p)-G_s\Xi_{s,t}(p),
 \qquad G_s\xi=(d_{A_s}\xi,0),\qquad
 J_{s,t}(w,p)=J_s^{\rm dec}w+H_{s,t}(p).
\]
Here $J_s^{\rm dec}$ is the decaying derivative of the exact
chart. Reading the primary classes at infinity and subtracting
$H_{s,t}$ proves that $J_{s,t}$ is a source isomorphism. It is
the derivative of an actual matching chart: solve the nonlinear
spatial $C$-Coulomb equation based at $B_e(s)$ and extend its
small gauge with the same cutoff. This leaves the base
configuration fixed and differentiates to the displayed formula.
The scalar stationary image vanishes, and all remaining scalar
columns belong to the weighted target. Equivariance gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-residual-gauge}
 \nabla\mathcal F_{c_s}G_s\Xi
       =\rho_*(\Xi)\mathcal F(c_s).
\end{equation}
This weighted equation column is retained; it need not vanish
at a nonsolution.

For Fredholmness, first work in physical decaying spaces. The
local primal estimates are \cite[Theorem~4.17 and Remark~4.19]{DFL};
the weak-adjoint estimates are \cite[Remark~4.33]{DFL} and
\cite[Theorem~5]{DFL-mixed}. Their boundary-domain
transport is \cite[Proposition~5.27 and Lemma~5.67]{DFL-mixed}.
The changed compact core is smooth at this fixed scale. At a
mixed stationary limit the current-center cylinder has the form
$\partial_\theta-\mathcal A_B$, with self-adjoint spatial
operator and a positive normal gap $\gamma$. Its weighted
whole-cylinder inverse satisfies
\[
 M_\delta\leq\frac1{\min(\delta,\gamma-\delta)},
 \qquad 0<\delta<\gamma,
\]
as well as the spatial first-order estimate. Stationary
coordinates are adjoined separately. Changing the scalar
center to $C$ adds a zeroth-order row of norm at most
$c\|C-B\|_{C^0}$. If $cM_\delta\|C-B\|_{C^0}<1/2$, a Neumann
series supplies the affine cylinder inverse and the inverse
of its actual adjoint. No self-adjoint spectral decomposition
for the affine operator is required. The true end coefficients
and matching domains converge to these models, so their errors
are absorbed after a common far cutoff. Ordinary end models
are unchanged. Compact-core center changes are relatively
compact by Rellich. Hence, after weight conjugation,
\[
 \|v\|_{H^1}\leq C(\|D^{\rm dec}_{s,t}v\|_2+\|v\|_{L^2(K)}),
 \quad
 \|y\|_{H^1}\leq C(\|(D^{\rm dec}_{s,t})^\dagger y\|_2
                                      +\|y\|_{L^2(K)}).
\]
Adding the finite stationary columns preserves Fredholmness.
Higher estimates and smooth finite stabilizations identify the
resulting determinant lines with the strong realizations.

For clarity, the adjoint here is taken after weight conjugation.
A zeroth-order physical center change leaves the principal Green
form and its matching annihilator unchanged. Write $K_{s,t}(p)=D_{s,t}(0,p)$ for the full target column
corresponding to the source lift $H_{s,t}$. A cokernel element
satisfies both $(D^{\rm dec}_{s,t})^\dagger y=0$ and
$K_{s,t}^*y=0$. A target map $S$
pulls the weak domain back by $S^*$; it does not leave the old
adjoint boundary label fixed. The source chart, which includes
a boundary trace extension, need not extend to a bounded map
on bulk $L^2$. We use it as a bounded isomorphism of Sobolev
domains, which preserves the cokernel annihilator. Equivalently,
complete its decaying source in the pulled-back norm
$\|J_s^{\rm dec}w\|_{L^2}$ before forming a coordinate adjoint.
Keep the finite stationary space as a separate summand.

There is also a target derivative. On the section factor choose
$S_c$ by pointwise parallel transport from $u(z)$ to $u_0(z)$
along the short geodesic in the target. The gauge target is
fixed. Shrinking the chart gives uniform normal neighbourhoods;
$S_{c_0}=1$ and $\nabla S|_{c_0}=0$. Pointwise multiplication
estimates preserve Paper~I's quadratic bound and initial
operator. Differentiating gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:affine-target-residual}
 d(S\mathcal F)_c[v]=S_c\nabla_v\mathcal F+R_cv,
 \qquad R_cv=(\nabla_v S)_c\mathcal F(c).
\end{equation}
The coefficient of $R_c$ tends to zero on the original ends,
where the residual tends to zero. It is compact from the
weighted decaying $H^1$ domain to $L^2$, by compact truncation
and Rellich, and its stationary columns are weighted finite
rank. The anchoring gives $R_{c_0}=0$ even at the nonsolution
preglue; also $R_{c_1}=0$ since $c_1$ is a solution. The target
map itself may have a nonidentity stationary limit.

The required square is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:full-affine-square}
 D_{s,t}=\left(S_{c_s}\nabla\mathcal F_{c_s}+(1-t)R_{c_s},
       \lambda^2d_{C_{s,t}}^{*,g}\pi_A\right)J_{s,t}.
\end{equation}
The preceding estimates prove its Fredholmness and continuity
everywhere. Its $t=0$ edge is exactly the derivative of the
contraction equation, including the affine scalar row and
\eqref{eq:affine-target-residual}. Paper~I's inverse and quadratic
bounds make this entire edge invertible. Strong invertibility
implies first-order invertibility: kernels regularize into the
strong domain, and a weak cokernel would annihilate every smooth
target reached by the strong inverse. Closed range and density
exclude that cokernel. At $s=0$, the centers and source maps
are constant and $R=0$, giving the invertible preglued operator
used in \eqref{eq:collapse-det}.

At $s=1$, equivariance gives $\nabla\mathcal F_{c_1}G_1=0$.
The scalar inverse splits the source into horizontal vectors
and decaying gauge directions. The resulting blocks are the
intrinsic quotient linearization and $P_{C_{1,t},A_1}$. Changing
\eqref{eq:affine-columns} changes only the representative of
that quotient tangent; \eqref{eq:affine-residual-gauge} now
vanishes. Regularity and index zero make the quotient block
invertible, and scalar coercivity makes the second block
invertible. This proves invertibility of the third edge.
At $t=1$ the residual term is absent and the center is $A_s$,
so the remaining edge is precisely the physical orientation
operator path. No invertibility assertion is made along its
interior.

Finally the required smallness follows from the actual
contraction bounds, not an unspecified fixed-scale neighbourhood.
On the unchanged original ends the spatial gaps and cylinder
constants are fixed. The stationary correction is bounded by
$C(1+|\log\epsilon|)^{m+1}e_\epsilon\to0$, with $e_\epsilon$
the pregluing error of Paper~I. Its strong-control estimate also
gives $\|A_s-A_0\|_{C^0(\widehat g)}\to0$. The temporal component
in this norm is the rescaled component, with no additional
$\epsilon^{-1}$ factor. The preglue's uniform scalar Poincare
estimate therefore persists, and both scalar and end
smallness conditions hold for sufficiently small $\epsilon$.
These facts do not give a uniform full mixed inverse at zero
scale. Compact parameter families use finite local covers;
actual gauge transitions transport both rows and all stationary
columns. Ordinary gluing at fixed positive scale commutes with
these identifications by the bordered determinant construction,
with corrections placed on the mixed factors and fixed ordinary
end models. No simultaneous independent neck-and-collapse
estimate is needed for this square.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:collapse-sign}
The map~\eqref{eq:collapse-det}, followed by transport
from $W_\epsilon(V)$ to $\mathcal G_\epsilon(V)$, preserves
the sign of the zero-dimensional solution.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Use the family \eqref{eq:full-affine-square} of
Proposition~\ref{prop:affine-square}. Its contraction edge is
invertible by the nonlinear estimate, its preglue edge is the
initial invertible collapse operator, and its solution edge is
invertible by the quotient and scalar block decomposition.
Lemma~\ref{lem:square} identifies the canonical orientations
along the remaining physical operator path. Composing this
identification with \eqref{eq:collapse-det} proves the claim.

That path, the edge $t=1$, is an extended realization, not an
admissible one in the sense of Definition~\ref{def:realization}: its
target map $S_{c_s}$ may have a nonidentity stationary limit, and its
source is the chart at $c_0$. It differs from an admissible realization
$L_{c_s}$ along the same configurations by bounded source and target
isomorphisms depending continuously on $s$. At $s=0$ these are the
identity, since $S_{c_0}=1$ and the chart is centred at $c_0$. The
naturality maps of determinant lines under these isomorphisms commute
with transport, and at $s=1$ both operators are invertible, so the
naturality map carries the canonical element to the canonical element.
Hence the identification along the edge $t=1$ is the one in the
admissible determinant bundle along $c_s$, which carries the
transported orientation.
\end{proof}

If the conformal metric in the collapse estimate is
changed within the unperturbed collar class, the zero
set of the mixed equation is unchanged. At a solution
Lemma~\ref{lem:realization} compares the gauge rows by
invertible paths, so the sign is unchanged. This permits
use of the metric of Paper~I without introducing a new
orientation convention.

The construction is compatible with compact parameter
families because its stabilizations and operator
bounds may be chosen locally uniformly. Associativity
of ordinary gluing identifies the determinant maps at
broken ends. This proves Theorem~\ref{thm:B}.

\subsection{Vertex identifications and two-handles}
In the triangle degeneration a completing vertex is
identified with a product of a gauge path and a
constant mixed pair. Normalize this identification
using the corresponding end frames, with the outgoing
coordinate translated in the positive direction on
every arm. The constant pair has sign $+1$ and the
gauge path uses the fixed object line. The resulting
vertex identification has sign $+1$ at the reference
configuration. Shifting and gluing show that it has
that sign throughout its configuration component.

For the standard surgery triple let $\theta_G$ denote
the distinguished gauge cycle in the auxiliary
connected sum of $S^1\times S^2$'s. Normalize its
symplectic counterpart by
\[
 \theta_S=N_{12}\theta_G.
\]
The unit diagonal and the usual filtration of that
auxiliary complex identify this with the distinguished
top cycle, with the chosen sign. The mixed triangle
family has one endpoint equal to a gauge triangle
followed by the mixed map, and the other endpoint
is a symplectic triangle with the two mixed inputs.
Proposition~\ref{prop:collapse-sign} compares the
counts at the collapse endpoint with the transported orientations,
and the two endpoints of the family are related by its oriented
boundary~\eqref{eq:family-boundary}. The broken boundary terms give a
homotopy, hence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:triangle-square}
 N_{02}\mu_G\simeq
 \pm\mu_S(N_{01}\otimes_R N_{12}).
\end{equation}
The gauge two-handle map is itself a triangle map over the integers.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:gauge-triangle-Z}
With the almost-complex orientations, the ordering of
Lemma~\ref{lem:tensor-geometric} and the conversions inserted as in
Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor},
\[
 \IG(W_2)\simeq\pm\mu_G(\,\cdot\otimes\theta_G),
\]
where $\mu_G$ is the framed gauge triangle map of the torus-summed
triangle (Paper~I, Section~9.3) and $\theta_G=\pm o_{q_*}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Paper~I, Proposition~9.5, proves the identity modulo two, and its
argument is integral once three points are checked. First,
$W_2^\#=X^\#\circ W'$ is cut along $Y_{01}^\#\sqcup Y_{12}^\#$, and the
connecting image in \cite[Proposition~5.4]{KM-unknot} vanishes by the
$H^0$ computation there. That proposition and the K\"unneth isomorphism
of \cite[Section~5.2]{KM-unknot} hold over $\ZZ$; the latter is written
in the left convention, and its transfer to the ordering of
Lemma~\ref{lem:tensor-geometric} is trivial on $a\otimes\widetilde\theta_G$,
because the invariant $\widetilde\theta_G$ of the filling is even in its
induced grading. Second, an element of
the lifting group with $s_{12}=1$ is realized by an automorphism
supported on the torus region. The almost-complex orientations are
invariant under all bundle automorphisms (Section~\ref{sec:gauge}), so
it maps solutions to solutions preserving their signs, and the orbit
count of Paper~I gives $\mu^{\{s_{12}=0\}}(a\otimes b)=\mu^\Phi(a\otimes\pi
b)$ over $\ZZ$, with no factor two. The conversion character of these
classes is trivial by Proposition~\ref{prop:character-gauge}, because
$J_W$ equals the fixed germ on the torus region. Third, the
conversions to the object bases are inserted and cancelled as in the
proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor}. Finally, the filling has
exactly one index-zero solution, with limit $q_*$ and invertible
operator (Paper~I, Proposition~9.4), so $\theta_G=\pm o_{q_*}$.
Paper~I, Proposition~9.5, and \cite[Proposition~5.4]{KM-unknot} are
stated on homology; the proof of the latter gives the composition at
chain level, up to the homotopy of Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor},
so the identity holds up to chain homotopy.
\end{proof}

The frame transports of Paper~I, Theorem~9.13, are pushforwards under
diffeomorphisms and bundle isomorphisms preserving the torus
decoration; their mixed squares are those of
Section~\ref{sec:descent}. Evaluating the second input
of~\eqref{eq:triangle-square} on $\theta_G$ and applying
Lemma~\ref{lem:gauge-triangle-Z} gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:twohandle}
 N_{p_1}\IG(W_2)\simeq\pm S_{W_2}N_{p_0}.
\end{equation}
Changing bases conjugates this identity by the
right-placed conversions and uses
\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}. Twisting the bundle on
the two-handle changes the gauge triangle class but
not the orientation argument; its relative bundle
is retained throughout the mixed family.

For simultaneous gluing at two triangle ends, use
one stabilization off both ends. The determinant
comparison is independent of the order of gluing,
up to the graded reordering already present in
\eqref{eq:tensor-maps}. Thus the triangle systems
are coherent at corners, which is needed when the
same product is later folded into a compression cup.

\section{Torus excision over the integers}\label{sec:excision}

\subsection{The closed cut and its orientation data}
Let $W_k:M\to M^+$ be the cobordism obtained by adding $k$
one-handles, where $M^+=M\#^k(S^1\times S^2)$. The folded triangle
for this compression has a closed gauge cut
\[
 Z\cong(-M)\#M^+\#T^3.
\]
The odd tori $T_a,T_b\subset Z$ are homologous, and the
lifting group at this cut is generated by
$s_Z=\PD[T_a]\pmod2$. Excision along these tori gives
\[
 Z'=(-M)^\#\sqcup(M^+)^\#.
\]
Write $\mathsf E:C_G(Z)\to C_G(Z')$ for the gauge
excision map and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ for its reverse.
The tensor complex at $Z'$ uses the right convention.

The almost-complex structure induced by the folded
triangle at $Z$ need not equal the one induced by the
mixed torus domain. A conversion at this interior cut
is therefore required. We choose the structures so that
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cut-Chern}
 c_1(J)[T_a]\equiv0\pmod4.
\end{equation}
For two such structures, the character
\eqref{eq:gauge-character} on $s_Z$ is trivial.
Consequently the conversion at $Z$ is defined on the
quotient complex.

The choice uses the topology of the particular fillings. In the
handle filling of Paper~I, Section~11.1, one has
\[
 B_k=(W_k\setminus\operatorname{int}\nu(\gamma))
           \natural(T^2\times D^2),
\]
and $T_a,T_b$ are fibres in the boundary of the displayed torus cap.
Choose the product almost-complex structure on the cap. Its first
Chern class evaluates to zero on both fibres. The boundary sum is
along a three-ball, so Mayer--Vietoris makes
$H^2(B_k;\ZZ)\to H^2(T^2\times D^2;\ZZ)$ surjective.
The relative obstruction argument of
Lemma~\ref{lem:almost-complex}, with the whole cap as the prescribed
region, extends this product structure: the primary obstruction
has zero connecting class and the secondary relative group in
degree four vanishes. The other end restrictions remain free.

On the excision side choose the same product germ on the
$T^2$ times polygon region of Paper~I, Section~10. The attachments
are away from the fibres at $T_a,T_b$. Write $A=T^2\times U$, where $U$ is the disk polygon of that
construction. Its complement is the disjoint union of
$[0,1]\times Q_-$ and $[0,1]\times Q_+$, with
$Q_\pm=(T^2\times I_\pm)\#M_\pm$. Each $Q_\pm$ has an integral
cohomology class restricting to the torus generator on both its
boundary tori. These classes match the generator on $A$ along
all four attaching faces. Mayer--Vietoris therefore gives
$H^2(\mathcal E;\ZZ)\to H^2(A;\ZZ)$ onto. Also $H^3(A;\ZZ)=0$
and $H^4(\mathcal E;\ZZ)=0$, so the secondary relative obstruction
vanishes. The same argument applies with the cobordism reversed. Make both structures cylindrical at the
closed cut, preserving their product germ near these two fibres.
Thus their Chern evaluations are actually zero.

Two end restrictions are prescribed. By convention the separate torus
domains $W_{\pm,\mathsf R}$ read their input lines $L_G(a_\pm)$ in the
base structures of $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$; this is unobstructed, since
their gauge regions deformation retract onto the input slice. Choose
$J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}$ with these base structures as its input
restriction at $Z'$; the two prescriptions agree on the collars of the
tori of $Z'$, where the object germ is the product germ. Then
choose $J_{\mathcal E}$ with restriction $J_{\overline{\mathcal E}}|_Z$ at
$Z$. Both extensions are unobstructed. Write $Q=Q_-\sqcup Q_+$, so that
the complement of $A$ is $[0,1]\times Q$. By excision,
\[
 H^*(\overline{\mathcal E},A\cup\partial_{\rm in}\overline{\mathcal E};\ZZ)
 \cong H^*\bigl((Q,\partial Q)\times([0,1],\{0\});\ZZ\bigr)=0,
\]
and likewise $H^*(\mathcal E,A\cup Z;\ZZ)=0$, so the primary and
secondary relative obstructions vanish. The product germ near the fibres
at $T_a,T_b$ is kept, so~\eqref{eq:cut-Chern} is unaffected. With these
choices the orientation maps of Section~\ref{subsec:torus-maps} and the
maps $N_\pm$ read their input lines in the same structures, and the
composite $\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf E$ has equal end structures at
$Z$. This is a choice
on the stated fillings and product regions; it does not require
a divisibility assertion for arbitrary tori in four-manifolds.

\begin{convention}\label{conv:interior-line}
The determinant line of the conversion at an interior
cut is placed after the complete input generator line
and before the complete configuration determinant line.
Its position is fixed relative to the entire
configuration, independently of where the geometric
cut is drawn.
\end{convention}

Suppose the configuration is written as a sequence of
glued pieces. Moving an odd conversion line with the
cut through that sequence would move it past the
determinant line of the intervening piece. The sign
would then depend on its index. Convention
\ref{conv:interior-line} avoids this dependence: moving
the line to a fixed reference position costs a sign
determined by the entire configuration, which is
unchanged by moving the cut. At index zero the
alternative fixed placement after the complete
configuration line agrees with this one.

\subsection{Lifting groups and gluing multiplicities}
It is useful to perform the gauge excision calculation
before passing to a quotient. Let $\mathcal G_0(X)$
be the subgroup of bundle automorphisms with trivial
lifting class. If a finite group $\phi_X$ acts freely
and orientation preservingly on the relevant
zero-dimensional solution set, its quotient count is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:orbit-count}
 I(X)^{\phi_X}(\bar a_0,\bar a_1)
 =\frac{1}{|\phi_X|}
   \sum_{a_i\in\bar a_i}\#\mathcal M(X;a_0,a_1).
\end{equation}
The numerator is divisible by $|\phi_X|$: it is a
sum of free orbits, each with a constant sign.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:gluing-multiplicity}
Let $X$ be connected and let $Y$ be a closed
hypersurface with cut manifold $X_Y$. Suppose the
critical limits on $Y$ have trivial stabilizer in the
adjoint determinant-one gauge group. For each matched
configuration on $X_Y$ that glues, the resulting
$\mathcal G_0(X)$-classes form a torsor for
\[
 K_Y=\ker\bigl(H^1(X;\ZZ/2)\longrightarrow
                         H^1(X_Y;\ZZ/2)\bigr).
\]
In particular their multiplicity is $|K_Y|$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Choose representatives whose two limits agree under
the fixed gluing identification. If two choices glue
to the same determinant-one class on the cut manifold,
their difference is an automorphism whose lifting
class restricts to zero on $X_Y$. Conversely every
such class changes the gluing while leaving the cut
class fixed. The stabilizer condition makes this action
free. Its transitivity follows by comparing the two
gluing identifications on the two copies of $Y$.
The Mayer--Vietoris sequence identifies $K_Y$ with the
image of the connecting map from $H^0(Y;\ZZ/2)$.
The matching and analytic assertions needed for the excision
cobordisms are proved below in
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}.
\end{proof}

A finite lifting group acts freely on the solutions
in the present calculation. Indeed, a fixed solution
would give a parallel automorphism. Its restriction
to an irreducible end must be the identity once its
lifting class there vanishes. A parallel automorphism
which is the identity on an end is the identity
everywhere on a connected cobordism. An element whose
class is nonzero at every end, such as $P_\pm$, restricts
to $s_Z$ at the $Z$ ends, and $\phi_Z$ acts freely on the
critical points of $Z$, so it fixes no solution either. The almost-complex
orientation construction preserves signs under the
whole group. Thus~\eqref{eq:orbit-count} applies.

\subsection{Ordinary self-gluing with regular cut data}
\label{sec:ordinary-selfgluing}

We prove Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing} using ordinary instanton
theory. Write $\mathcal E$ for the cobordism defining $\mathsf E$ and
put $V=\mathcal E\cup_{Z'}\overline{\mathcal E}$. The local polygon
description gives a nonseparating cut $\Sigma=T^2\times\gamma$;
its complement $V^\circ$ is connected and retains the original
$Z$ ends. The bundle is odd on the torus fiber. The other composite
has a separating torus cut. All components of the cobordisms that
we regularize have an original cylindrical end; the standard caps
are kept fixed. The geometric cap comparison and the larger lifting
groups will be described in the next subsection.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}]
\emph{Perturbations and relative regularity.}
Keep the entire ordinary end functional fixed, including the
trajectory perturbation in $\mathrm{CS}_h+f$ from Paper~I.
Only the secondary cobordism perturbations vary. On each new
three-torus use a fixed flat product metric and the unperturbed
equation, throughout all the comparison families below. The odd
bundle has two determinant-one flat critical points, both acyclic.
The class $\PD[T^2\times\{\mathrm{point}\}]$ interchanges them;
their relative grading is four modulo eight
\cite[Proposition~5.2 and Lemma~5.3]{KM-unknot}. Thus the critical
points have the same parity and the required lifting action is free.

Every nonconstant unperturbed torus trajectory has full index
at least four. Indeed, the two end Hessians are conjugate by an
adjoint gauge transformation, so their spectral correction terms
agree. The cylinder index and energy are $8\kappa$ and
$8\pi^2\kappa$, respectively, with $\kappa\in\frac12\ZZ$.
Positive energy therefore implies index at least four. The required
compactifications have total dimension at most two. After joint
regularization of their cut pieces, a torus trajectory of index at
least four would leave a negative augmented index for the remaining
pieces, counting the data parameter once. Such a stratum is empty.
Consequently no torus trajectory perturbation is needed in this
dimension range. We do not assert regularity of the higher-index
unperturbed torus trajectories. The caps use these same unperturbed
end data and have no secondary perturbation.

Choose a product collar joining each component to an autonomous
end, and a compact interval $J$ in that collar. The metric is fixed
there, including in parameter families. If $F_Y$ is the image on
the collar cross-section $Y$ of the allowed lifting group, use
secondary functionals
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-secondary}
 q_s(B)=\sum_\ell c_\ell\psi_\ell(s)h_\ell(B),
 \qquad \operatorname{supp}\psi_\ell\subset J,
\end{equation}
where the $h_\ell$ are $F_Y$-invariant holonomy cylinder functions
on $Y$. Their holonomy loops lie in a single slice. Elements of
the kernel of the restriction to $F_Y$ impose no further condition.
Use a countable dense collection of profiles and cylinder functions,
with a weighted Banach completion controlling all required smooth
norms. The holonomy estimates of
\cite[Propositions~3.7 and~3.15]{KM-instantons} persist: time
derivatives multiply the corresponding profile bounds, and the
derivative in $B$ is bounded on $L^2$ on bounded sets of data.

This allows sums of compact time profiles in the secondary
perturbation construction of~\cite[Section~3.7]{KM-unknot}.
For the compactness estimate, include the fixed primary cutoff
terms in $q_s$. In a consistent gradient normalization,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-energy}
 \frac{d}{ds}(\mathrm{CS}+q_s)(B(s))
 =-\|\dot B(s)\|^2+\partial_s q_s(B(s)).
\end{equation}
The additional integral is bounded by the $L^1$ norm of the time
derivatives of the coefficients, uniformly under stretching.
Its support remains compact. The spatial holonomy estimates,
local regularity and autonomous middle-neck equation are the same
as in the ordinary broken-metric construction. No perturbation
depends on connections on both sides of a cut.

We give the transversality argument because averaging a generic
perturbation does not by itself prove invariant regularity.
First, slice continuation holds for these time-dependent collar
equations. On a compact slab write the equation for a difference
or an adjoint field as
\[
 (\partial_s+H(s)+K(s))u=0.
\]
Here $H$ is formally self-adjoint on a common smooth domain,
$H'$ is $L^2$-bounded and $K$ is $L^2$-bounded. For the one-form
equation, $H=\pm *d_{B(s)}$; the connection derivative is order
zero. The difference of two nonlinear holonomy terms has this
bounded form by integration of their derivatives along the segment
between the two connections. For a compactly supported smooth $v$
and a concave weight $\varphi$ with $-\varphi''\ge c>0$,
\begin{align*}
 \|(\partial_s+H+\lambda\varphi')v\|^2
 &=\|v'\|^2+\|(H+\lambda\varphi')v\|^2
   -\int\langle v,H'v\rangle
   -\lambda\int\varphi''\|v\|^2.
\end{align*}
Absorbing $K$ gives, for large $\lambda$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-continuation}
 (c\lambda-C)\|e^{-\lambda\varphi}u\|^2
 \le 2\|e^{-\lambda\varphi}(\partial_s+H+K)u\|^2.
\end{equation}
Choose $\varphi$ increasing on a short slab. If a solution vanishes
before a slice, cut it off farther into the slab; the cutoff error
has strictly larger $\varphi$ than a neighborhood of the slice.
Letting $\lambda$ tend to infinity proves vanishing in that
neighborhood. Reversing $s$ proves the opposite continuation.
Vanishing on a whole slice suffices, since the equation then makes
all time derivatives vanish there and extension by zero is smooth.
This estimate does not require ellipticity of $*d_B$ on all
one-forms.

Suppose an allowed automorphism fixes a slice of a solution. Extend
it constantly in temporal gauge. Invariance and slice continuation
make it fix the entire end and its limiting critical point.
Freeness of $F_Y$ at that point forces its lifting class to vanish;
irreducibility then makes its even lift central. Thus each collar
slice has a free $F_Y$ orbit and trivial effective even stabilizer.
The cylinder-function density in the proof of
\cite[Proposition~3.10]{KM-instantons} detects every nonzero smooth
direction transverse to gauge there. It also does so invariantly:
take a small finite-dimensional slice through the direction and
its finitely many disjoint translates, prescribe equivariant
first jets on their union, approximate by cylinder functions, and
average. Freeness ensures that the prescribed derivative survives.

Let $L_A$ be the linearized perturbed ASD equation. A cokernel
element for $(d_A^*,L_A)$ is a pair $(\xi,b)$, with $b$ self-dual.
Gauge equivariance at a solution gives $L_A d_A=0$, hence
$d_A^*d_A\xi=0$. The $L^2$ end condition and integration by parts
give $\xi=0$. On the collar write $b$ using a one-form $y(s)$.
The remaining adjoint equations give
\[
 d_{B(s)}^*y=0,\qquad
 -\partial_s y+*d_{B(s)}y+K(s)y=0.
\]
If this cokernel annihilates every secondary variation, then
\[
 \int_J\psi(s)\langle y(s),\nabla h(B(s))\rangle\,ds=0
\]
for every time profile and invariant cylinder function. Time
localization, invariant density and the Coulomb equation imply
$y=0$ on $J$. Equation~\eqref{eq:ordinary-continuation} propagates
this to the unperturbed core. Local unique continuation for the
ordinary ASD adjoint gives vanishing on the connected core, and
slice continuation handles its other collars. This proves
surjectivity of the universal linearization. All nonlocal terms
in this argument are supported on product end collars; spatial
unique continuation through nonlocal interior terms is not used.

Sard--Smale now supplies invariant regular data. For a broken
configuration with pieces indexed by $i$ and one parameter $b\in B$,
the joint linearization is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-joint-family}
 ((\xi_i)_i,\dot b)\longmapsto
       (D_i\xi_i+\partial_b\mathcal F_i\dot b)_i.
\end{equation}
Independent secondary variations on the pieces kill every component
of a joint cokernel by the preceding argument. There is only one
$T_bB$ summand. Choose regular data on fixed faces first, extend over
their collars, and use parameter-dependent variations supported
away from them. Intersect the residual sets for the finitely many
cut patterns and countably many relative classes. Fixed regular
caps require no variation. This gives the relative regular families
of~\cite[Proposition~3.10 and Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} in the invariant
class just constructed.

\emph{Matching on the prescribed bundle and finiteness.}
An even gauge transformation is a section of the bundle with fiber
$\SU(2)$ and conjugation structure action; no lift of the underlying
$\SO(3)$ bundle is required. The fiber is 2-connected, and
conjugation acts trivially on $\pi_3$. If gauge values are prescribed
on boundary components $B_0$ of a compact cobordism $W$, the sole
extension obstruction is in
\[
 H^4(W,B_0;\ZZ)\cong H_0(W,\partial W\setminus B_0;\ZZ).
\]
It vanishes when every component has an unprescribed boundary end.
Apply this to the two new boundaries of $V^\circ$, leaving its
original $Z$ ends free. Equal critical classes can therefore be
represented by equal connections under the prescribed gluing
identification, with allowed even changes at the original ends.
They glue on the specified global bundle. For capping, keep the
cap representatives fixed and make the changes on $V^\circ$.

Components of the even gauge group on a connected closed
three-manifold are classified by $H^3(Y;\ZZ)=\ZZ$. On a connected
$W$ the obstruction for prescriptions on its whole boundary is
the signed sum
\[
 \bigoplus_{Y\subset\partial W}H^3(Y;\ZZ)
      \longrightarrow H^4(W,\partial W;\ZZ)\cong\ZZ.
\]
It is onto, with kernel the extendible prescriptions. Thus relative
classes for a fixed tuple of critical end classes form a
$\ZZ$-torsor, whose generator changes the ASD index by eight.
Every such class can be represented on the fixed bundle in this
nonsingular setting~\cite[Section~3.7]{KM-unknot}. Central
stabilizers introduce no additional degree. A fixed index and tuple
therefore allow at most one relative class. The critical sets are
finite. In a regular family, each component index is bounded below
by minus the base dimension; at fixed total dimension this leaves
finitely many index allocations. The shared parameter is counted
once in the total dimension.

The final secondary data may be finite sums in
\eqref{eq:ordinary-secondary}. On a bounded neighborhood of data,
\eqref{eq:ordinary-energy} and the ordinary index-energy relation
bound the index below. The preceding class calculation makes the
bounded index ranges finite. The regular low-dimensional
compactifications have regular gluing neighborhoods at their broken
strata. Surjectivity is open on their finite union: a sequence of
nonregular solutions for convergent data would limit either to a
regular point or to a regular broken configuration, contradicting
the local estimate in either case. Finite sums are dense, also
for smooth coefficient families on a compact base. Approximate
within this open set, relative to the prescribed face collars.
This preserves the original end equations and all supports.

\emph{Local gluing and exhaustion.}
At an irreducible nondegenerate critical connection $\beta$, the
extended scalar-plus-one-form Hessian $B_\beta$ is invertible.
Its spectral gap gives a uniform inverse for
$\partial_s+B_\beta$ on the full cylinder. A regular index-zero
cut solution $A$ has an invertible full operator $D_A$.
For self-gluing, patch its inverse on the connected cut piece,
including both stubs, with the cylinder inverse. Cutoffs with
derivatives $O(T^{-1})$ give an approximate right inverse $R_T$ and,
separately, the estimate
\[
 \|D_{A_T}R_T-1\|\le C(T^{-1}+\eta_T),\qquad
 \|u\|_{H^1}\le C\|D_{A_T}u\|_{L^2}
                    +C(T^{-1}+\eta_T)\|u\|_{H^1},
 \quad \eta_T\longrightarrow0.
\]
There is one copy of $D_A$, rather than one for each stub.
Compactly supported secondary terms and slice-local primary terms
commute with these cutoffs. The full inverse is uniformly bounded
for large $T$. Exponential pregluing error and the uniform quadratic
estimate in a sufficiently high Sobolev norm give a unique nearby
solution in the Coulomb slice. Stabilization gives the same charts
and determinant maps for regular universal families.

Use the broken-metric compactification argument of
\cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot}, with empty singular locus.
Its definition permits a nonseparating cut, and the odd torus
satisfies the required non-integral condition. On the new torus
ends we use the index exclusion above in place of regularity of
all trajectories: any nonconstant torus trajectory removes at
least four from the index available to the regular joint cut
operator. This remains impossible in the two-parameter corner
families. The retained strata have regular original-end
trajectories, regular joint cut operators and acyclic matching
limits, exactly the inputs to the local compactification and
gluing arguments. In total dimension
at most one, the cut solutions form the broken-metric boundary;
further neck breaking has higher codimension, and bubbling is
excluded by low-dimensional compactness. The local gluing charts
give its collars. Finiteness of the union over relative classes
then gives one neck threshold: any additional solutions at lengths
tending to infinity would converge to a boundary point and hence
belong to its gluing collar.

For disjoint cuts use the multi-parameter corner model; the
two-parameter index-zero family has dimension two, still below the
source's bubbling threshold. A simultaneous stretching of two
components is the diagonal in the two gluing
parameters; the product gluing collars restrict to its collar.
Equivalently the two-edge composition cycle is obtained by one
separating gluing followed by one self-gluing. The cuts $\Sigma$
and $Z'$ intersect, so their comparison instead joins finite
stretching parameters by a relative regular path. It does not
stretch intersecting cuts simultaneously.

\emph{The lifting fiber.}
Compare the even lifts on the components of a cut manifold. Their
values at paired ends agree up to central signs. Changing a lift
by the center on one component changes the incident signs by the
graph coboundary. The remaining choices form the first cohomology
of the cut graph over $\FF$, identified with $K_Y$ by
Mayer--Vietoris. These classes have actual gauge representatives.
For a connected cut, choose a unit section $n$ of the adjoint
bundle on $Y$ and, on its normal collar, use the adjoint of
\[
 \cos(\pi t)+\sin(\pi t)n,\qquad 0\le t\le1,
\]
with $t$ constant near the endpoints. The section exists because
the Euler class of an oriented rank-three bundle is 2-torsion
and $H^3(Y;\ZZ)$ is torsion-free. The adjoint transformation is
identity at both ends, while its lift runs from $+1$ to $-1$;
its lifting class is $\PD[Y]$.

The action is transitive by comparison of the lifts. It is free:
a fixed solution would give a parallel automorphism, even on an
original irreducible end, hence identity there and everywhere.
The almost-complex determinant orientations preserve this action.
Thus the multiplicities are two for the nonseparating $\Sigma$
cut and the two-edge $Z'$ composition cycle, and one for a
separating cut or a cap tree.

\emph{Trace signs and homotopies.}
First use the induced almost-complex structures at the ends, with
the orientation torsors of~\cite[Section~5.1]{KM-unknot}.
Apply its Proposition~5.5 with the enlarged group exactly
$K_\Sigma=\langle\PD[\Sigma]\rangle$. This group restricts trivially
to $V^\circ$, to $\Sigma$ and to the original ends. The trace
therefore uses the full determinant-one complex on $\Sigma$.
The free orientation-preserving action just proved recovers the
raw count as twice that trace. The larger quotient is taken only
after this step.

The alternating sign here is an analytic determinant sign, as in
the proof of~\cite[Proposition~5.5]{KM-unknot}, which refers to
\cite[Lemma~2.4]{KM-sutures}. In the periodic
model $\partial_s+B$, a simple real eigenvalue crossing changes
the real zero Fourier mode once; the nonzero modes occur in
complex pairs. The sign is spectral-flow parity, hence the parity
of the critical generator, with one fixed reference normalization.
Excision localizes the comparison to the neck. The cut operator
and any stabilization occur on both sides; continuous changes of
cutoffs introduce no further sign. Internal basis changes cancel,
and the index-eight change of relative class preserves the complex
normalization. This explains why there is no independent sign
choice for each generator or relative class.

With the traced factor last, write $F_{jj}$ for the diagonal block
of a degree-$f$ map $F$ on a tensor end with torus basis $g_j$.
The right tensor convention gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:ordinary-right-trace}
 \operatorname{Tr}_R(F)=\sum_j(-1)^{(f+1)|g_j|}F_{jj}.
\end{equation}
For $f=0$ this is the alternating trace just normalized. Moving
the traced line past the degree-$f$ map supplies the additional
$(-1)^{f|g_j|}$. Conjugation by the tensor isomorphism $T$ of
Section~\ref{sec:algebra} gives the same formula algebraically.
The trace commutes with the Hom differential and satisfies
\[
 \operatorname{Tr}_R((U\otimes_R1)F(V\otimes_R1))
       =U\operatorname{Tr}_R(F)V.
\]
Consequently the external conversion maps can be inserted with
their actual degrees. Both torus generators have one parity $p$,
so comparison with unit cap coefficients $u,v\in\{1,-1\}$ has
factor $(-1)^{(f+1)p}/(uv)$, up to the fixed overall orientation.
Interior conversions retain Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}.

Finally use the parameter-first orientation of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}, with outward-normal-first boundary
orientations. The source convention in
\cite[Section~3.9]{KM-unknot} is fiber-first. For an $r$-parameter
family the permutation of its determinant factors contributes
\[
 (-1)^{r\operatorname{ind}D}.
\]
For a zero-dimensional one-parameter count the vertical index
is $-1$, so this changes the sign of the count. For the universal
one-dimensional space the vertical index is zero, so its orientation
and its endpoint maps are unchanged. For two parameters the
permutation sign is one; boundary faces use the same one-parameter
rule.

We include the separate generator-order conversion. At the induced
end gradings the unconverted cobordism maps $F_i$ are even. Write
$d_0$ and $H_0$ for the source differential and its fiber-first
homotopy count, so its oriented boundary formula is
$F_1-F_0=d_0H_0+H_0d_0$. Placing an odd trajectory determinant
after its input generator changes $d_0$ to $d=d_0\eps$ and
$H_0$ to $H_0\eps$. The parameter-first change contributes the
additional minus sign, so the homotopy operator in our convention is
\[
 K=-H_0\eps,\qquad
 dK+Kd=d_0H_0+H_0d_0=F_1-F_0.
\]
Here $\eps$ is the parity operator on the appropriate source
complex; the calculation uses that $d_0$ and $H_0$ are odd.
On disconnected ends the whole ordered input generator line gives
the tensor rule~\eqref{eq:tensor}. Thus the parameterized boundary
formula agrees with~\eqref{eq:family-boundary}. Arbitrary external
conversions, including odd ones, are then governed by the Hom
Leibniz rule. All these choices are uniform across matrix entries.
This proves all three assertions in the stated orientation
convention.
\end{proof}

\subsection{The capped comparison}\label{subsec:capped}
Let $V=\mathcal E\cup_{Z'}\overline{\mathcal E}$ be
the composite of excision and reverse excision.
The standard local model is $T^2$ times the doubled
excision polygon. It contains a nonseparating
hypersurface $\Sigma=T^2\times\gamma$. Cut along
$\Sigma$ to obtain $V^\circ$, and cap the two new
boundary components by $T^2\times D^2$. The capped
cobordism $V'$ is a product on $Z$; this is the
geometric comparison in torus excision
\cite[Theorem~5.6]{KM-unknot}.

The determinant-one complex on $\Sigma$ has two
critical points $g_1,g_2$. The class
$a=\PD_\Sigma[T^2]$ interchanges them. They must be
kept separate when the caps are attached: the caps
carry the trivial lifting group, since $a$ does not
extend across their meridional disks.

Write $s=\PD_Z[T_a]\pmod2$. In $H^1(V^\circ;\ZZ/2)$
let $p$ be its pullback and $h_1,h_2$ the meridional
classes of the two removed cap neighborhoods. The
restrictions are
\[
\begin{array}{c|cccc}
 &Z_{\rm in}&Z_{\rm out}&\Sigma_{\rm in}&\Sigma_{\rm out}\\\hline
 p&s&s&0&0\\
 h_1&s&0&a&0\\
 h_2&s&0&0&a\\
 h_1+h_2&0&0&a&a
\end{array}
\]
The group $H^\circ=\langle p,h_1,h_2\rangle$ has order
$8$. It contains both groups needed on the cut
cobordism, but is not itself the pullback of either
of the groups on $V$ and $V'$.

More precisely, $\phi_V$ has order $8$, its image on
$V^\circ$ is $\langle p,h_1+h_2\rangle$ of order $4$,
and its kernel is $K_\Sigma$ of order $2$. The capped
product has group $\phi_{V'}=\langle p\rangle$ of
order $2$. To check these assertions, the two sides
of the excision polygon give classes $P_+,P_-$ and
the disconnected intermediate cut gives a class $c$.
Their restrictions are $p,p,h_1+h_2$, respectively.
They are independent on $V$, as seen by loops
crossing each of the two polygon sides and the
intermediate cut. Their sum $P_++P_-$ is dual to
$\Sigma$, generates the restriction kernel, and is
nonzero because $\Sigma$ is nonseparating.

Choose invariant regularizing data on $V^\circ$.
Let
\[
 D(\alpha,\sigma;\beta,\tau)
 =\#\mathcal M(V^\circ;(\alpha,\sigma),(\beta,\tau)),
\]
where $\sigma,\tau\in\{g_1,g_2\}$. Put
$\Delta(\alpha,\beta)=D(\alpha,g_1;\beta,g_1)$.
The action of $h_1+h_2$ makes the two diagonal
blocks equal; the action of $h_2$ identifies the
off-diagonal block with $\Delta(s\alpha,\beta)$.
Thus, after summing over endpoint lifts, diagonal
and off-diagonal blocks have the same sum.

We verify the cap count for the unperturbed torus data chosen in
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}. Complete $T^2\times D^2$ by a
product cylindrical end, giving $T^2\times S$, where $S$ is a
complete disk with a cylindrical end. Give $T^2$ a flat metric
and use a product metric on the cap. Let $A_0$ be the pullback
of the odd flat connection on $T^2$. Its adjoint local system
on $T^2$ is acyclic: it splits into the three nontrivial real
sign local systems. The torus Hodge Laplacian therefore has a
positive lower bound in every degree. The product Hodge Laplacian
on $T^2\times S$ has the same lower bound, since its two summands
are nonnegative.

For the full flat operator $D_0=d_{A_0}^*\oplus\sqrt2d_{A_0}^+$,
the identities
\[
 D_0^*D_0=\Delta_{A_0,1},\qquad
 D_0D_0^*=\Delta_{A_0,0}\oplus\Delta_{A_0,2}^+
\]
show that its kernel and cokernel vanish. Elliptic estimates and
the positive gap give an inverse on the cylindrical Sobolev
spaces. Thus the flat cap has index zero and is regular.

Call its boundary lift $g_1$. The other lift $g_2$ has relative
grading four modulo eight, whereas changing a relative class
at a fixed lift changes the cap index by eight. Hence an
index-zero cap can approach only $g_1$, in the zero-charge class
of $A_0$. Its unperturbed ASD energy is $8\pi^2\kappa=0$,
so it is flat. The flat odd torus connection has a unique flat
extension across the disk in the prescribed bundle: the disk
meridian is contractible and $\pi_1(T^2\times S)=\pi_1(T^2)$.
The two boundary lifts distinguish whether the meridional
determinant-one lift extends; only $g_1$ does. Consequently there
is exactly one determinant-one cap class. Its signed coefficient
is a unit, and the reversed cap gives the corresponding dual
unit. This calculation is at the full two-generator torus
complex, before any lifting-group quotient.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:capped}
For the regular data of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}, the
