quotient counts for $V$ and $V'$ have the same absolute
multiplicity. In each fixed degree they agree up
to the determinant-gluing sign.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For $V$, self-gluing has multiplicity $2$ and there
are two equal diagonal blocks. Quotienting by the
group of order $8$ gives
\[
 \frac{2\cdot2}{8}
   \sum_{\alpha\in\bar a,\,\beta\in\bar b}
       \Delta(\alpha,\beta).
\]
For $V'$, the caps have unit coefficients, their
separating gluings have multiplicity $1$, and the
group has order $2$. The result is
\[
 \frac12
   \sum_{\alpha\in\bar a,\,\beta\in\bar b}
       \Delta(\alpha,\beta).
\]
If the second cap selects $g_2$, the symmetry of
the off-diagonal block gives the same sum. The
orientation signs are those in
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}. All group actions preserve the
orientations, so no cancellation occurs inside
an orbit. The two numerical factors agree.
\end{proof}

In particular no division by two is performed on
a Floer homology group. The fractions above express
integer orbit counts. Computing the caps only
in the rank-one quotient would lose the distinction
between matching two lifts and matching two orbits.

The one-dimensional gluing family identifies these
counts with the composite excision map. The same
argument for the other composite gives the reverse
comparison. There the cut is the separating torus cut
$T^2\times\gamma'$, so there is no trace and the matching
multiplicity is one. The lifting group has order eight, against
the capped group $\langle s_-,s_+\rangle$ of order four. On each cut
piece the meridional class of the removed cap exchanges
$\sigma\in\{g_1,g_2\}$ and moves one endpoint lift within its orbit,
so after summing over endpoint lifts the two matched choices
contribute equally. This gives a factor two, and the factors
balance: $2/8=1/4$. Consequently $H(\mathsf E)$ and
$H(\overline{\mathsf E})$ are isomorphisms. With the
end structures prescribed in Section~\ref{sec:excision},
$\mathsf E$ and $\overline{\mathsf E}$ have the same structure at
$Z$; at $Z'$ the composite is formed with the right-placed
conversion, as at the fixed end bases. By
Proposition~\ref{prop:gauge-functor} it equals the map of $V$ for any
admissible structure on $V$, after the end conversions, up to one
sign for all entries. The unconverted map of $V^\circ$ is even, so
$f=0$ and the capped factor is $(-1)^p/(uv)$; hence the composite
is $\pm1$ on every homogeneous class, and the conversion at $Z'$ has
even total parity. This is the only inverse-excision statement
needed for the handle state below. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:acyclic}, the closed excision maps are
homotopy equivalences (after shifting degrees if necessary).

\subsection{The torus domains and their orientation lines}
\label{subsec:torus-lines}
The remainder of this section proves the integral mixed excision
theorem. Its unsigned content is Paper~I, Section~10: the separate
collared domains $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ ($a=\pm$, $0\le\mathsf R<\infty$),
their completions $W_{a,\infty}$, the joined domains $W^J_\lambda$
($\lambda>0$), the loss formula, the torus-completion bijections at
large length, the collar and width families, and the closed-cut
decomposition. We use those results as they are proved there. The
domain list of Section~\ref{sec:geometry} includes the finite-width
torus domains, their completed limits and the closed-cut mixed
domain, so Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations} and
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} apply to them and to
the compact families below. What we add is an orientation for every
count and every one-dimensional family, and the resulting chain
homotopy over $\ZZ$. No further analytic assumption is made.

At $\mathsf R=0$ the separate domain is the original special
quintuple, and its count is the vertex map $N_a$. For every
$\lambda>0$ the closed cut of Paper~I, Lemma~10.18, decomposes the
joined domain as
\[
 W^J_\lambda=\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda\cup_Z M_\lambda .
\]
Here $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ is the reverse excision cobordism of
Kronheimer and Mrowka, with inputs $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$, and
$M_\lambda$ is a mixed domain of DFL type for the pair $(A_0,A_1)$,
with compression bodies $D_{0,\lambda},D_{1,\lambda}$ and single
ordinary input $Z$. The lifting group is the fibre product
$\Phi_J=\phi_{\overline{\mathcal E}}\times_{\phi_Z}\phi_M$ over
$\phi_Z=\langle s_Z\rangle$.

\emph{Labels and lines.} An input label is an ordered pair
$a=(a_-,a_+)$ of critical points on $(-M)^\#$ and $(M^+)^\#$, with
generator line $I_a=L_G(a_-)\otimes L_G(a_+)$. An output label is a
point
\[
 x=(x_-,x_+)\in A_0\cap A_1
   =(\widetilde P_0\cap\widetilde P_1)\times(R_0\cap R_1).
\]
Let $C_{S,-}$ and $C_{S,+}$ be the target complexes of $N_-$ and
$N_+$, with their transported orientation systems, and let
$L_\pm(x_\pm)$ be their generator lines. Put
\[
 V_x=L_-(x_-)\otimes L_+(x_+),\qquad
 C_I=C_G((-M)^\#)\otimes_RC_G((M^+)^\#),\qquad
 C_V=C_{S,-}\otimes_RC_{S,+}.
\]
In Section~\ref{sec:cup} these are $\CF(\bar p)$ and $\CF(q)$.

Choose the invariant ordinary datum $f_Z$ on $Z$ to vanish on a
neighbourhood of the entire critical set. The determinant-one critical classes on $Z$ over $x$ are exactly
two, as in \cite[Lemmas~2.43 and~2.45]{DFL}: the two genus matching
components permit two central signs, and a constant central gauge on
either compression piece changes both, which leaves $\{1,-1\}^2$
modulo its diagonal. An $\SO(3)$ arrow of lifting class
$s_Z=\PD[T_a]\pmod2$, the projection of $\cos(\pi\rho)+\sin(\pi\rho)n$
on a genus collar, exchanges them, and it acts freely
(Paper~I, Lemma~10.18(iv), for the lifting classes). Both are irreducible and nondegenerate
\cite[Lemma~2.47]{DFL}, so the quotient critical set is
$A_0\cap A_1$. Let $\beta_x$ be either
lift. The complex $C_Z=\CF_X(A_0,A_1)$ is the target of $N_Z$; its
generator line over $x$ is
\[
 Z_x=L_G^{M}(\beta_x),
\]
the gauge line of $\beta_x$ in the almost-complex structure of the
mixed piece. The two lifts have lines identified by the action of
$s_Z$, as in Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}.

Finally, let $Q_x$ be the conversion line at $\beta_x$ from the
structure induced by $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ to that of $M_\lambda$, and
let $t$ be its parity. By Section~\ref{sec:gauge}, $t$ does not
depend on $x$. Choose one orientation of $Q$ at a base connection
and transport it along paths of connections on $Z$. Take the structure of $M_\lambda$ with the product germ near the
fibres $T_a,T_b$ on its torus region, as in Section~\ref{sec:excision},
so that \eqref{eq:cut-Chern} holds for both structures at $Z$. By
Proposition~\ref{prop:character-gauge}, the transport therefore gives
coherent orientations $q_x$ of all $Q_x$, invariant under $\phi_Z$. The composite $N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}$
always means the composite through the right-placed conversion
$\Theta^{\mathrm r}$ at $Z$, with its line in the position of
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}; it has degree $t$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:CM-invertible}
Let $C_{M,x}$ be the primary constant pair on $M_\lambda$ with limit
$\beta_x$ on its ordinary input and with no secondary or
inhomogeneous data. Its physical operator, with stationary columns and
scalar row, is invertible for every finite $\lambda>0$ and along the
family in the proof of Paper~I, Lemma~9.7, as applied in Paper~I,
Proposition~10.21, which changes the finite torus-neck lengths and the
compact metric of $M_{\lambda_0}$ to those of the special quintuple of
$(D_0,D_1)$. The inverse bounds are uniform on compact
subfamilies.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
This is part of Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs} in
Appendix~\ref{app:inputs}: the kernel vanishes by the quadratic identity
of \cite[Lemma~4.40]{DFL} adapted to these domains, and the index is
zero by \cite[Proposition~3.19]{DFL} at the special quintuple and
continuity along the family. The proof of that lemma uses the local
theory of Section~\ref{app:local}, Lemma~\ref{lem:compact-remainder},
Paper~I, Theorem~10.13, Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii) and
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}; none of the inputs of that lemma uses the
present lemma.
\end{proof}

The determinant line of an invertible operator has the canonical
positive element $1$, and conjugation by a gauge transformation
preserves it.

\subsection{Orientation maps on the torus domains}
\label{subsec:torus-maps}
All orientation maps below are defined up to positive scalars and
take values in the lines just described.

\emph{Separate maps.} For a configuration $c$ on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$,
$0\le\mathsf R\le\infty$, Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} gives
\[
 \Gamma^a_c:L_G(a_a)\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow L_a(x_a).
\]
For a configuration $c$ on the closed-cut piece $M_\lambda$, with
ordinary input $\beta$, the same proposition, with the positive
orientation of Lemma~\ref{lem:CM-invertible} at the terminal constant
pair, gives
\[
 \Gamma^M_c:L^M_G(\beta)\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow Z_x .
\]

\emph{Strip actions.} For $b\in\{-,+,M\}$ and a regular strip $u$
from $x$ to $y$ at the output of the corresponding domain, glue $u$ to
the constant pair at $x$ and evaluate $\Gamma^b$ on the positive
constant element. This defines an even map
\[
 R_b(u):L_b(x)\otimes\det D_u\longrightarrow L_b(y),
\]
with $L_M(x)=Z_x$. Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} gives the
coherence
\begin{equation}\label{eq:strip-coherence}
 \Gamma^b_{c\#u}\bigl(i\otimes\kappa(d\otimes e)\bigr)
 =R_b(u)\bigl(\Gamma^b_c(i\otimes d)\otimes e\bigr),
\end{equation}
where $\kappa$ is ordered determinant gluing. The differentials of
$C_{S,\pm}$ and $C_Z$ count index-one strips with these actions. For
a split product strip $u=(u_-,u_+)$ put $n_-=\ind D_{u_-}$ and
\begin{equation}\label{eq:RV}
 R_V(u)(v_-\otimes v_+\otimes e_-\otimes e_+)
 =(-1)^{|v_+|n_-}\,
   R_-(u_-)(v_-\otimes e_-)\otimes R_+(u_+)(v_+\otimes e_+).
\end{equation}
The sign is the move of $\det D_{u_-}$ past $L_+(x_+)$. A regular
index-one product strip for split data has exactly one nonconstant
factor, of index one, since each nonconstant factor has a translation
vector in its kernel and a constant factor at a transverse
intersection is invertible. Thus \eqref{eq:RV} gives the right
tensor differential \eqref{eq:tensor} on $C_V$.

\emph{The decorated closed-cut map.} Call a configuration on
$W^J_\lambda$ \emph{terminal} if it agrees with a constant pair $C^c_{M,x}$,
for some representative $c$ of the critical point over $x$, on the part of
$M_\lambda$ beyond a parallel copy of $Z$ in the product collar of the cut,
and on a two-sided collar of that copy. Its remaining factor $e$, the
reverse excision cobordism enlarged by the collar, is an arbitrary
configuration with the given inputs. Ordinary gluing at the copy of $Z$
identifies its determinant line with $\det D_e\otimes\det D_{C^c_{M,x}}$,
and the lines at $c$ are identified with those at $\beta_x$ as in
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}. Let
$\gamma_e:I_a\otimes\det D_e\to L^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_G(\beta_x)$ be the
almost-complex orientation of the gauge cobordism, with the ordered
inputs, and let
$\Theta_x:L^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_G(\beta_x)\otimes Q_x\to Z_x$ be the
conversion. Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:G-terminal}
 G_c(i\otimes q\otimes d_e\otimes1)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_e}\,\Theta_x\bigl(\gamma_e(i\otimes d_e)\otimes q\bigr).
\end{equation}
The sign moves $Q_x$ from its fixed position after the input line to
the position adjacent to the cut. For a general configuration $c$ on
$W^J_\lambda$, the localized several-input shift of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants} and Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}
retains the ordinary cobordism and reaches a terminal configuration. Transport \eqref{eq:G-terminal}
back along it. This defines an even map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:G-map}
 G_c:I_a\otimes Q_x\otimes\det D_c\longrightarrow Z_x .
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:G-properties}
The map \eqref{eq:G-map} is independent of the shifting path, natural
under $\Phi_J$, and continuous on compact families of finite widths.
It has the following compatibilities.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item If $A$ is an ordinary path at the input, then
\[
 G_{A\#c}\bigl(i\otimes q\otimes\kappa(d_A\otimes d)\bigr)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_A}G_c\bigl(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes q\otimes d\bigr).
\]
\item If $u$ is a regular strip at the output, from $x$ to $y$, then,
with the coherent orientations $q_x,q_y$,
\[
 G_{c\#u}\bigl(i\otimes q_y\otimes\kappa(d\otimes e)\bigr)
 =R_M(u)\bigl(G_c(i\otimes q_x\otimes d)\otimes e\bigr).
\]
\item If $c=A\#_Zc'$ is an ordinary gluing at the closed cut of a
configuration $A$ on $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ from $a$ to $\beta$ and a
configuration $c'$ on $M_\lambda$ from $\beta$ to $x$, then, with
the coherent orientations $q=q_x$ and $q_\beta$,
\[
 G_c\bigl(i\otimes q\otimes\kappa(d_A\otimes d')\bigr)
 =(-1)^{t\ind D_A}\,
   \Gamma^M_{c'}\bigl(\Theta_\beta(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes q_\beta)
                        \otimes d'\bigr).
\]
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Path independence is the square argument in the proof of
Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}, with the relative localized shift of
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(b). Apply it to a path whose endpoints
are terminal. The endpoint tracks and the final edge remain terminal,
where \eqref{eq:G-terminal} is continuous. The orientation double
cover over the parameter square then shows that the original path
preserves \eqref{eq:G-terminal}. Applying this to the concatenation
of two shifting paths from $c$ proves independence. The lifting arrows preserve
the almost-complex orientations, the conversion character on
$\phi_Z$ and the positive constant element, and they map terminal
configurations to terminal ones. The pullback of a shifting path by an
arrow is a shifting path, so path independence shows that they preserve
\eqref{eq:G-map}.

For (i), the path $A$ is attached at an original input, away from the
localized shift. At a terminal configuration, ordinary associativity
gives $\gamma_{A\#e}(i\otimes\kappa(d_A\otimes d_e))
=\gamma_e(\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\otimes d_e)$, and the two sides of
(i) carry the exponents $t(\ind D_A+\ind D_e)$ and
$t\ind D_A+t\ind D_e$. Continuity along the shift gives (i).

For (ii), glue $u$ to the whole shifting family of $c$ with one
long neck. At the terminal end the configuration is $e\#C^c_{M,x}\#u$;
shift $C^c_{M,x}\#u$ to $B\#C^{c'}_{M,y}$ with $B$ an ordinary path on $Z$
of index $n=\ind D_u$, and insert a long product neck at $Z$ so that
$B$ lies on the $\overline{\mathcal E}_\lambda$ side beyond a two-sided
collar of $Z$; then $e\#B\#C^{c'}_{M,y}$ is terminal. The ordinary action of $B$ in the two
structures satisfies the line form of the right conversion
convention \eqref{eq:Theta}:
\[
 \gamma^M_B\bigl(\Theta_x(v\otimes q_x)\otimes d_B\bigr)
 =(-1)^{tn}\Theta_y\bigl(\gamma^{\overline{\mathcal E}}_B(v\otimes d_B)\otimes q_y\bigr).
\]
The right side of (ii) at the terminal configuration therefore
carries the exponent $t\ind D_e+tn$. The left side places $Q$ before
the determinant of $e\#B$, with exponent $t(\ind D_e+n)$. The other
factors agree by associativity and the definition of $R_M(u)$.
Continuity gives (ii).

For (iii), shift $c'$ on $M_\lambda$ to $B\#C^{c}_{M,x}$ and glue $A$ to
the whole family at $Z$ with one long neck. Both sides of (iii) are
continuous orientations of the same line along this family. At its
end the configuration $A\#B\#C^{c}_{M,x}$ is terminal, and by
\eqref{eq:G-terminal} its left side carries
$(-1)^{t(\ind D_A+\ind D_B)}$. On the right,
$\Gamma^M_{B\#C^{c}_{M,x}}$ is the action of $B$ in the structure of
$M_\lambda$, which by the display above contributes
$(-1)^{t\ind D_B}$; the prefactor contributes $(-1)^{t\ind D_A}$.
The two sides agree at the end, hence along the family.
\end{proof}

\emph{The reference comparison.} Let $C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ be the
joined constant pair on $W^J_\lambda$ with output $x$. Its incoming
labels are the critical points $a_{x_\pm}$, so its input line is
$L_G(a_{x_-})\otimes L_G(a_{x_+})=V_x$. Its operator is invertible for
every $\lambda$ (Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iv) below). Evaluating \eqref{eq:G-map} on
the positive element gives an even isomorphism
$G_{\mathrm{ref}}:V_x\otimes Q_x\to Z_x$. Define the even map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:K0}
 K_{0,x}:Z_x\longrightarrow Q_x\otimes V_x,\qquad
 K_{0,x}G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes q)=(-1)^{t|v|}q\otimes v .
\end{equation}
The lines are fixed and $G_{\mathrm{ref}}$ varies continuously with
$\lambda$, so $K_{0,x}$ does not depend on $\lambda$. Replacing
$C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ by the joined constant pair of another representative
conjugates the operator by a gauge transformation. It is assembled from the
automorphisms relating the representatives on the two sides; these are
central on $T$, and after multiplying one of them by $-1$ they agree there
and glue across the bridge. The positive elements correspond, and so do the
identified lines, by the remark on representatives in
Appendix~\ref{app:shifting}; hence $K_{0,x}$ is unchanged. Let $K^q_x$ be
\emph{literal} contraction of the first factor with $q_x$: if
$K_{0,x}(z)=q_x\otimes v$, then $K^q_x(z)=v$. It has degree $t$. Put
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KR-def}
 \mathcal K=K^q,\qquad \mathcal K_R=K^q\eps_{C_Z}^t .
\end{equation}
Literal contraction is intended; the right tensor of the odd map
$q_x^*$ with the identity would insert an additional Koszul sign.

\subsection{The split orientation and completion}
\label{subsec:torus-split}
On the completed pair $W_{-,\infty}\sqcup W_{+,\infty}$ the product
of the separate maps is
\begin{equation}\label{eq:S-infty}
 S_{(c_-,c_+)}(i_-\otimes i_+\otimes d_-\otimes d_+)
 =(-1)^{|i_+|\ind D_{c_-}}\,
   \Gamma^-_{c_-}(i_-\otimes d_-)\otimes\Gamma^+_{c_+}(i_+\otimes d_+).
\end{equation}
We extend it to large finite widths through the torus cutoff of
Paper~I. On the far torus tail of a completed side write a
configuration as $\alpha+b$ in the torus-fibre slice of Paper~I,
Lemma~10.9. A compact family is \emph{in torus normal form beyond
$r_0$} if every member has this form for $r\ge r_0$, with $b$ bounded
in every weighted norm and with the weighted norms of $b$ on $r\ge r_1$
tending to zero uniformly as $r_1\to\infty$. For a pair $c=(c_-,c_+)$
in torus normal form beyond $r_0$ and $\lambda\ge2r_0+2$, the preglue
$P_\lambda(c)$ replaces $b$ by $\chi_\lambda b$ on both tails, with
$\chi_\lambda(r)=\chi(r-\lambda/2)$, and joins them by $\alpha$ on the
bridge, as in Paper~I, Lemma~10.24. On one side the truncation
$P_{\mathsf R}(c_a)$ on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ is defined in the same way; it
equals $\alpha$ near the face and so satisfies the flat-fibre
condition.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:split-preglue}
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item Every compact family on the completed domains, in the topology of
Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, is in torus normal form beyond some $r_0$
after a continuous family of small even gauge transformations which
are the identity for $r\le r_0-1$ and on the ordinary ends.
\item Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs in torus normal form
beyond $r_0$. The residual of $P_\lambda(c)$ relative to $c$ is supported
where $\chi_\lambda'\neq0$ or $\chi_\lambda^2\neq\chi_\lambda$, and tends to
zero in every weighted norm, uniformly on $\mathfrak F$, as
$\lambda\to\infty$. The same holds for $P_{\mathsf R}$ on one side.
\item The constant pairs $C_{a,x_a}=C^{c_a}$ whose representatives $c_a$
are $\alpha$ on $T$, such as those produced in Appendix~\ref{app:shifting},
are equal to $\alpha$ on the whole added tail. Then
$P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x_a})$ is the constant pair
on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$, and
$P_\lambda(C_{-,x_-},C_{+,x_+})=C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$ is the joined constant
pair.
\item The operators at these constant pairs are invertible for every
$\mathsf R$ and every $\lambda$.
\end{enumerate}
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
(i) Continuity and compactness make the set of tail differences
$A-\alpha$ compact in $H^m_\delta$. Their norms on $r\ge r_1$ therefore
tend to zero uniformly as $r_1\to\infty$. Sobolev embedding on unit
boxes, whose geometry is fixed on the flat tail, makes them uniformly
small in $C^1$ beyond some $r_1$. Paper~I, Lemma~10.9, then gives a
unique small even gauge transformation into the torus-fibre slice on
$r\ge r_1$, continuous in the family. Cut off its logarithm on
$[r_1,r_1+1]$, as in Paper~I, Lemma~10.11, and take $r_0=r_1+1$. The
resulting gauges are connected to the identity by the path of
logarithms, so determinant transport along them is canonical, and
every orientation map below is natural under them.

(ii) Only the tail is changed. There the residual is
$(d\chi_\lambda\wedge b)^++(\chi_\lambda^2-\chi_\lambda)(b\wedge b)^+$, which is
bounded by the norms of $b$ on $r\ge\lambda/2-1$.

(iii) Choose the stationary representatives equal to $\alpha$ on the
whole torus region of the slice, not only on the far collar. For a
stationary representative $B=B_T+B_r\,dr$, flat on the torus band
$[b,R]$ and equal to $\alpha$ near $r=R$ (Paper~I, Proposition~10.7),
solve $\partial_rg=-B_rg$, $g(R)=1$, on a slightly larger interval
$[c,R]$ in the determinant-one gauge bundle. Then $B^g$ has no radial
component there, and flatness gives $B^g=\alpha$ on $T\times[b,R]$. With
a cutoff $\eta$ equal to zero near $c$ and one near $[b,\infty)$, the
gauges $q_t(r)=g\bigl(R+t\eta(r)(r-R)\bigr)$, $t\in[0,1]$, form a path
from the identity, supported in a fixed collar, with
$B^{q_1}=\alpha$ on $T\times[b,\infty)$. They preserve primary classes
and genus germs, and depend smoothly on compact families of primary
data. The constant pairs are pullbacks of these representatives, so
they equal $\alpha$ on the tail and the truncation and the preglue have
no cutoff error; the results are the constant pairs on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$
and $W^J_\lambda$, which are solutions.

(iv) This is Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}.
\end{proof}

The next lemma is the linear gluing across the torus bridge. The
bridge is not an ordinary end, since its cross-section
$T\times\RR_\theta$ runs into the mixed ends, so
Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} does not apply to it.

\begin{lemma}[Determinant lines across torus completion]
\label{lem:bridge-gluing}
Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs in torus normal form
beyond $r_0$. For $c\in\mathfrak F$ let $D_\lambda(c)$ be the physical
realization at $P_\lambda(c)$ and $D_\infty(c)=D_{c_-}\oplus D_{c_+}$.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item There are a finite-dimensional space $E$, a linear map
$\Lambda:E\to Y$ into smooth target sections supported in a fixed
compact subset of the two cores, and $\lambda_1$, such that
$D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda$ is onto, with a uniformly bounded right
inverse, for all $c\in\mathfrak F$ and $\lambda\in[\lambda_1,\infty]$.
If every $D_\infty(c)$ is onto, one may take $E=0$.
\item The kernels of $D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda$ form a continuous vector
bundle over $\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$; in particular
$\ind D_\lambda(c)=\ind D_\infty(c)$ for $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$. Near $\lambda=\infty$ its
local frames are $\iota_\lambda(v)=\beta_\lambda v-Q_\lambda(D_\lambda\oplus
\Lambda)\beta_\lambda v$, where $\beta_\lambda$ is multiplication by
$\chi_\lambda$ on both tails and $Q_\lambda$ is the right inverse of (i).
\item Consequently the lines $\det D_\lambda(c)$ form a continuous line
bundle over $\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$ whose fibre at $\lambda=\infty$
is $\det D_{c_-}\otimes\det D_{c_+}$. This structure does not depend on
$\Lambda$. Where the operators are invertible, the canonical positive
elements form a continuous section.
\item Let an ordinary path or strip be glued, with a long neck, at an
original gauge input or at the strip output, to every member of
$\mathfrak F$. The identification of (iii) commutes with the ordinary
gluing isomorphism of Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing}.
\end{enumerate}
The same statements hold for a compact family in torus normal form on
one completed side and its truncations $P_{\mathsf R}(c)$ on
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$, $\mathsf R\to\infty$. They also hold for any other
family of operators of the same form: the chart operators of Paper~I,
Definition~10.23, and the interpolating operators in the proof of
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-path}, since the proof uses only the properties
listed at its beginning.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Compact-remainder estimates.} Steps~1--3 of the proof of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.26, use only the following: on the torus region beyond
a fixed $S$ the operator is the flat odd-torus operator $D_\alpha$ of
Paper~I, Proposition~10.3, plus a small zeroth-order term, with the
normal derivatives at a flat face recovered as in the proof of Paper~I,
Theorem~10.5(b); on the mixed ends it is transported to the stationary
models by the maps of \cite[Proposition~5.27]{DFL-mixed}, with errors
tending to zero, and the slice estimates of Paper~I, Proposition~10.7
and Corollary~10.4, hold; the ordinary asymptotic operators are
invertible; local elliptic estimates hold on a fixed compact region;
the coefficients are bounded in $C^m$ on unit patches of finitely many
isometry types; and the weight satisfies $\delta<\gamma/2$,
$\delta<\mu_T$, with $\tau=\tau_0(\theta)$ on the torus region. The
smallness beyond $S$ follows from the uniform tails in the definition
of torus normal form and Sobolev embedding; by the local estimates and
end models of Section~\ref{app:local}, and compactness of $\mathfrak F$,
these hold uniformly on $\mathfrak F\times[2r_0+2,\infty]$. The finite
stationary columns and the scalar row of the physical realization are
bounded, and they act through the fixed cores and the mixed ends.
The same argument therefore gives
\[
 \|x\|\le C\bigl(\|D_\lambda(c)x\|+\|x\|_{L^2(K_1)}+|p(x)|\bigr),
\]
where $K_1$ is a fixed compact subset of the two cores and $p(x)$ the
stationary coordinates. The adjoint of $D_\alpha$ is invertible with the
same bound, the stationary models are self-adjoint, and the local
estimates for the weak adjoint are recorded in
Section~\ref{app:local}. Hence the same steps give
\[
 \|\eta\|\le C\bigl(\|D_\lambda(c)^*\eta\|+\|\eta\|_{L^2(K_1)}\bigr)
\]
on the dual weighted spaces.

(i) At $\lambda=\infty$ each $D_\infty(c)$ is Fredholm by
Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}(a). Smooth compactly
supported target sections are dense, so finitely many of them span a
complement of its range, and by continuity also near $c$. A finite
cover of $\mathfrak F$ gives $\Lambda$; if every $D_\infty(c)$ is onto,
no sections are needed and $E=0$. Suppose there were $\lambda_j\to\infty$,
$c_j\in\mathfrak F$ and $\eta_j$ with $\|\eta_j\|=1$ and
$\|(D_{\lambda_j}(c_j)\oplus\Lambda)^*\eta_j\|\to0$. By the adjoint estimate,
$\|\eta_j\|_{L^2(K_1)}\ge1/C$. Pass to $c_j\to c$. As in Step~5 of that
proof, the parts of $W^J_{\lambda_j}$ within distance $\lambda_j/2$ of each
core are identified with the corresponding parts of $W_{\pm,\infty}$,
and local elliptic bounds give a subsequence converging weakly on
compact sets and strongly in $L^2(K_1)$ to some $\eta_\infty$ on the
completed pair. Then $(D_\infty(c)\oplus\Lambda)^*\eta_\infty=0$ and
$\eta_\infty\neq0$, which contradicts the choice of $\Lambda$. Hence
$\|\eta\|\le C\|(D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda)^*\eta\|$ for
$\lambda\ge\lambda_1$, and
$Q_\lambda=T^*(TT^*)^{-1}$, with $T=D_\lambda(c)\oplus\Lambda$ and $T^*$
its Hilbert adjoint for the weighted inner products, is a uniformly
bounded right inverse; the estimate for the formal adjoint on the dual
weighted space is equivalent to one for $T^*$ through the Riesz
isometry, and it is proved first at the $H^1$ level and then upgraded
by the elliptic estimates of Step~3. The map $Q_\lambda$ is continuous in $(c,\lambda)$ for finite
$\lambda$.

(ii) At $\lambda=\infty$ the kernels form a vector bundle over
$\mathfrak F$, since the operators are onto. Its unit sphere bundle is
compact, so the tails of its elements on $r\ge r_1$ are uniformly small
as $r_1\to\infty$. Hence
$\|(D_\lambda\oplus\Lambda)\beta_\lambda v\|\to0$ uniformly for unit $v$:
the error consists of $\sigma(d\chi_\lambda)v$ and of the coefficient
difference on the tail, both supported where $r\ge\lambda/2-1$. Thus
$\iota_\lambda$ is injective for large $\lambda$ and converges to the
identity. It is onto: otherwise unit kernel elements orthogonal to
its image would, by the estimate for $D_\lambda$ and the argument in (i),
converge to a nonzero kernel element at $\lambda=\infty$ orthogonal to
the whole limiting kernel. For finite $\lambda$ the kernels of the
surjective family form a bundle as usual.

(iii) The exact sequence
$0\to\ker D\to\ker(D\oplus\Lambda)\to E\to\operatorname{coker}D\to0$
identifies $\det D$ with $\det\ker(D\oplus\Lambda)\otimes(\det E)^*$,
and (ii) makes these lines continuous. At $\lambda=\infty$ the ordered
direct-sum isomorphism gives $\det D_{c_-}\otimes\det D_{c_+}$. Two
choices $\Lambda,\Lambda'$ are compared through $\Lambda\oplus\Lambda'$ by
the standard stabilization maps, which are continuous in $(c,\lambda)$
because they are built from the same kernel bundles. On an invertible
operator the stabilization identification carries the canonical
element to the canonical element, so the positive elements are
continuous where the operators are invertible.

(iv) Choose $\Lambda$ supported away from the ordinary gluing neck as
well as from the torus bridge; this is possible because the sections
spanning the cokernels in (i) have compact support in the cores, which
lie at a fixed distance from both necks once they are long. Then the
glued operators with both necks long form a family over the product of
the two neck-length parameters, including their limits. The kernel
transplants of (ii) and of Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} act by cutoffs
and projections in disjoint regions, and the estimates of (i) hold
uniformly on this product. The resulting determinant bundle is
continuous over the product of the two closed length intervals, which
is contractible. The two orders of gluing therefore give the same
identification.

For one side use the half-plane and strip models of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.3, with the flat-fibre face, in place of the bridge.
\end{proof}

Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family of pairs of configurations on the
completed domains. By Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) we may assume it
is in torus normal form. Examples are a pair of index-zero solutions
and a pair of constant pairs with strips glued at their outputs. For
$c\in\mathfrak F$ define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:S-def}
 S_{P_\lambda(c)}:I_a\otimes\det D_{P_\lambda(c)}\longrightarrow V_x ,
 \qquad\lambda\ge\lambda_1,
\end{equation}
as the continuous extension of \eqref{eq:S-infty} along
$\{P_\lambda(c)\}_{\lambda\in[\lambda_1,\infty]}$ given by
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}. It is continuous on
$\mathfrak F\times[\lambda_1,\infty]$ and natural under the joined lifting
group $\Phi_J\cong\Phi_-\oplus\Phi_+$.

The separate shifts of Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting} give, for
$c\in\mathfrak F$, a homotopy $h\mapsto H_h(c)$, $h\in[0,1]$, continuous in
the topology of Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, from $c$ to
$(A_-\#C^{c_-}_{-,x_-},A_+\#C^{c_+}_{+,x_+})$ with $A_\pm$ ordinary paths
and representatives $c_\pm$ which are $\alpha$ on $T$. Its image
is compact. By Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) it can be put in torus
normal form beyond a common $r_0$, and by
Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii) its endpoints equal $\alpha$ on the
tails. Applying \eqref{eq:S-def} to this larger compact family gives
$S$ on the rectangle $\{P_\lambda(H_h(c))\}$. At $\lambda=\infty$ it is
\eqref{eq:S-infty}, which along $h$ is the transport defining
$\Gamma^\pm$ in Proposition~\ref{prop:transport}. At $h=1$ the
configuration is $(A_-\sqcup A_+)\#C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$, and there $S$ is
the ordinary orientation of $A_-\sqcup A_+$ followed by the positive
element of the invertible constant factor, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iii),(iv). The rectangle is
contractible, so this description of $S$ at $P_\lambda(c)$ through $h=1$
does not depend on the chosen shifting homotopy.

The same device compares the separate orientations on the finite and
completed domains. By construction, $\Gamma^a$ on the finite domain
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$ is a continuous orientation over its configuration
space, defined by the one-input shift on that domain.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:separate-completion}
Let $\mathfrak F$ be a compact family on $W_{a,\infty}$ in torus normal
form. For $\mathsf R\ge\mathsf R_1$, the orientation $\Gamma^a$ of
$W_{a,\mathsf R}$ at $P_{\mathsf R}(c)$ is the continuous extension of
$\Gamma^a_c$ along $\{P_{\mathsf R}(c)\}_{\mathsf R\in[\mathsf R_1,\infty]}$
given by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Consider the rectangle $\{P_{\mathsf R}(H_h(c))\}$, with $H_h$ the
separate shift in torus normal form. The extension from
$\mathsf R=\infty$ is continuous on it by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}.
For fixed finite $\mathsf R$ the orientation $\Gamma^a$ of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$
is continuous in $h$. At $h=1$ the configuration is
$A\#P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x})$, and $P_{\mathsf R}(C_{a,x})$ is the constant
pair of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ by Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(iii). There
$\Gamma^a$ is the ordinary orientation of $A$ followed by the positive
element of the constant factor. The extension from $\mathsf R=\infty$ is
the same: by Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) it commutes with gluing
$A$, and the constant factor is invertible for every $\mathsf R$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}) with
positive element continuous up to $\mathsf R=\infty$.
The two orientations therefore agree at $h=1$, and hence on the whole
rectangle.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}\label{cor:strip-actions}
The strip actions $R_\pm(u)$ computed on $W_{\pm,\mathsf R}$ do not depend
on $\mathsf R\in[0,\infty]$, and $R_M(u)$ computed on $M_\lambda$ does not
depend on $\lambda$ and agrees with the action on the special quintuple
of $(D_0,D_1)$ defining $N_Z$. Thus each of $C_{S,\pm}$ and $C_Z$ carries
one orientation system.
\end{corollary}
\begin{proof}
Each action is an isomorphism between fixed lines, defined through an
invertible constant pair with a strip glued at its output. For finite
$\mathsf R$ it varies continuously with $\mathsf R$, since the constant
pairs are invertible for every $\mathsf R$
(Lemma~\ref{lem:constant-pairs}); at $\mathsf R=\infty$ use
Lemma~\ref{lem:separate-completion} with $\mathfrak F=\{C_{a,x}\#u\}$,
which is $\alpha$ on the tail, together with
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) for the strip glued at the output. For $R_M$ use the width interval and the
family of Paper~I, Lemma~9.7, along which $C_{M,x}$ stays invertible
by Lemma~\ref{lem:CM-invertible}.
A continuous family of isomorphisms between fixed lines is constant.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:reference-comparison}
On the family $P_\lambda(H_h(c))$, $c\in\mathfrak F$, for $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$
and $h\in[0,1]$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:reference-comparison}
 K_{0,x}G_c=(1_{Q_x}\otimes S_c)(\sigma_{I,Q}\otimes1_{\det}),
\end{equation}
where $\sigma_{I,Q}$ moves $Q_x$ past the entire input line. For the
literal insertion $G^{\mathrm r}_c(i\otimes d)=G_c(i\otimes q_x\otimes d)$
and $m=\ind D_c\pmod2$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:KR-G}
 \mathcal K\,G^{\mathrm r}_c=(-1)^{t|I|}S_c,\qquad
 \mathcal K_R\,G^{\mathrm r}_c=(-1)^{t(m+1)}S_c .
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Both sides of \eqref{eq:reference-comparison} are continuous on the
rectangle at finite $\lambda$, so it suffices to verify it at $h=1$.
There the configuration is $(A_-\sqcup A_+)\#C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$.
Let $h_A$ be the total parity of $\det D_{A_-}\otimes\det D_{A_+}$ and
let $v=\gamma_A(i\otimes d_A)\in V_x$, so $|v|=|i|+h_A$. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(i),
$G_c(i\otimes q\otimes\kappa(d_A\otimes1))
=(-1)^{th_A}G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes q)$, and by \eqref{eq:K0}
\[
 K_{0,x}G_c(i\otimes q\otimes\kappa(d_A\otimes1))
 =(-1)^{th_A+t|v|}q\otimes v=(-1)^{t|i|}q\otimes S_c(i\otimes d).
\]
This is the right side of \eqref{eq:reference-comparison}. For the
second assertion, the tensor of maps $1_Q\otimes S_c$ with even $S_c$
introduces no sign, so literal contraction gives
$\mathcal KG^{\mathrm r}_c=(-1)^{t|I|}S_c$. Since $G_c$ is even,
$|G^{\mathrm r}_c(i\otimes d)|=|i|+t+m$, and $\eps^t$ contributes
$(-1)^{t(|i|+t+m)}$. The product is $(-1)^{t(t+m)}=(-1)^{t(m+1)}$.
\end{proof}

At index zero the coefficient in \eqref{eq:KR-G} is $(-1)^t$; at the
isolated points of a one-parameter family, where $m$ is odd, it is
$+1$. The index-zero sign must not be applied to homotopies.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:KR-closed}
$\mathcal K_R:C_Z\to C_V$ is an isomorphism of graded groups, and
\[
 d_V\mathcal K_R=(-1)^t\mathcal K_Rd_Z .
\]
Thus $\mathcal K_R$ is a closed isomorphism of degree $t$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
$K_{0,x}$ is an isomorphism of lines, and the generators of $C_Z$ and
$C_V$ are both indexed by $A_0\cap A_1$; hence $\mathcal K_R$ is an
isomorphism. Let $u=(u_-,u_+)$ be a regular index-one product strip
from $x$ to $y$ for the split data. Apply
Proposition~\ref{prop:reference-comparison} to the pair of separate
configurations $(C_{-,x_-}\#u_-,C_{+,x_+}\#u_+)$, which is in torus
normal form, and to its preglue $C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}\#u$. By
\eqref{eq:strip-coherence} on each side and \eqref{eq:S-infty}, the
split orientation at $\lambda=\infty$ is
\[
 S_{C_J\#u}\bigl(v\otimes\kappa(1\otimes e)\bigr)=R_V(u)(v\otimes e).
\]
Indeed, $\Gamma^\pm_{C_\pm}(v_\pm\otimes1)=v_\pm$, the determinant of
$C_{\pm}\#u_\pm$ is $\kappa(1\otimes e_\pm)$ with
$\ind D_{C_-\#u_-}=n_-$, and \eqref{eq:strip-coherence} gives
$\Gamma^\pm(v_\pm\otimes\kappa(1\otimes e_\pm))=R_\pm(u_\pm)(v_\pm\otimes e_\pm)$.
Formula \eqref{eq:S-infty} with $i_\pm=v_\pm$ then contributes
$(-1)^{|v_+|n_-}$, which is the sign in \eqref{eq:RV}; reordering the
even constant elements costs nothing. By continuity in $\lambda$, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing}(iv) for the strip glued at the output,
the identity holds at large finite widths. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(ii) with $c=C^{(\lambda)}_{J,x}$,
\[
 G_{C_J\#u}\bigl(v\otimes q_y\otimes\kappa(1\otimes e)\bigr)
 =R_M(u)\bigl(G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes q_x)\otimes e\bigr).
\]
Apply $K_{0,y}$ and use \eqref{eq:reference-comparison} for
$C_J\#u$ and \eqref{eq:K0}. Both sides of the resulting identity,
evaluated on $G_{\mathrm{ref}}(v\otimes q_x)\otimes e$, equal
$(-1)^{t|v|}q_y\otimes R_V(u)(v\otimes e)$. Let $J_u:Q_x\to Q_y$ be the conversion transport along the ordinary
path obtained by shifting $C_{M,x}\#u$; by coherence $J_uq_x=q_y$.
Hence
\[
 K_{0,y}R_M(u)=(J_u\otimes R_V(u))(K_{0,x}\otimes1_{\det D_u}),
\]
with $Q$ to the left of both $V$ and the strip determinant; since
$R_V(u)$ is even, the tensor of maps inserts no sign. Literal
contraction with $q_x,q_y$ gives
$K^q_yR_M(u)(z\otimes e)=R_V(u)(K^q_xz\otimes e)$. Summing over
index-one strips gives $d_V\mathcal K=\mathcal Kd_Z$. Since
$d_Z\eps^t=(-1)^t\eps^td_Z$, the assertion for $\mathcal K_R$ follows.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Signed counts at large torus length}
\label{subsec:torus-counts}
Fix regular data as in Paper~I, Section~10.11, steps (1)--(4). The
completed index-zero sets are finite and regular, there are no
completed solutions of negative index, and for
$\mathsf R\ge\mathsf R_0$, $\lambda\ge\lambda_1$ Paper~I,
Theorem~10.31, gives the bijections
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}$ and $\mathcal G_\lambda$ onto regular index-zero
solutions, with no solutions of negative index. Write
$N_{a,\infty}$ for the completed separate counts, oriented by
$\Gamma^a$, and $N_a^{(\mathsf R)}$ for the counts on $W_{a,\mathsf R}$.
On $W^J_\lambda$ let $F^S_\lambda:C_I\to C_V$ count index-zero
solutions oriented by $S$, and $F^Z_\lambda:C_I\to C_Z$ the same
solutions oriented by $G^{\mathrm r}$; their degrees are $0$ and $t$.
The orientation $S$ at these solutions is defined by the following
lemma.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:gluing-path}
Let $V$ be a completed index-zero solution, or a pair of them, and for
$\mathsf L\ge\mathsf L_1$ let $z_{\mathsf L}(V)$ be the small zero of
Paper~I, Proposition~10.29, so that
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)=\Psi_{\mathsf L}(z_{\mathsf L}(V))$. Consider
the rectangle of configurations
\[
 \mathcal R_V=\{P_{\mathsf L}(V):\mathsf L\in[\mathsf L_1,\infty]\}
 \cup\{\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L}(V)):s\in[0,1],\
                  \mathsf L\in[\mathsf L_1,\infty)\},
\]
with $P_\infty(V)=V$, where $V$ is represented by the adapted
representative of Paper~I, Lemmas~10.11 and~10.24, so that the two edges
meet; the gauge of Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) is then the
identity, by fibrewise uniqueness in Paper~I, Lemma~10.9. The physical
determinant lines form a continuous line bundle over $\mathcal R_V$, and determinant transport carries the
canonical positive element at $V$ to the canonical positive element at
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Over the first edge the line bundle is that of
Lemma~\ref{lem:bridge-gluing} with $\mathfrak F=\{V\}$, and its
extension clause covers the chart operators used below; the second edge is a
continuous family on a fixed domain for each $\mathsf L$.

Use also the chart operators $T$ of Paper~I, Definition~10.23. At the
preglues they are invertible with a uniform bound by Paper~I,
Proposition~10.26; at $\mathsf L=\infty$ the chart is centered at $V$
and the operator is invertible by Step~4 of its proof and index zero.
Along the contraction path the chart operator is
$D_{\mathsf L}+DN_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L})$ with
$\|DN_{\mathsf L}(sz_{\mathsf L})\|\le2C_{\rm quad}r\le(2C_{\rm inv})^{-1}$,
as in the proof of Paper~I, Proposition~10.29, so it is invertible.
The chart operator at $\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz)$ is centered at the preglue.
Moving its slice center, trace chart and target projection to the
configuration itself changes it by at most
$C(\|sz\|+\|D\Psi_{\mathsf L}(sz)-1\|)$ in operator norm, which is small,
so the straight path between them consists of invertible operators.
The resulting operator and the physical realization at the same
configuration have the same decaying block; their stationary columns
and scalar rows differ by a bounded finite-rank map (Paper~I, remark
after Definition~10.23). The straight path between these two is
therefore a continuous Fredholm path, and these identifications are
continuous over $\mathcal R_V$. The positive elements of the invertible
chart operators thus give a continuous nonvanishing section of the
physical determinant bundle.

At the two solutions $V$ and $\mathcal G_{\mathsf L}(V)$ this section is
the canonical positive element. Indeed, there the change of stationary
columns is unipotent: the chart lifts and the Coulomb lifts differ by
infinitesimal gauge directions $d\psi_e$ and by decaying fields already
in the domain (Paper~I, Step~4 of the proof of Proposition~10.26), and a
unipotent change of source coordinates preserves the positive element.
The change of the scalar row is block triangular with invertible gauge
block, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:realization}, using Paper~I,
Lemma~10.25. At these points every operator of the interpolating paths
is invertible. No kernel at a nonsolution of $\mathcal R_V$ is
identified with a tangent space.
\end{proof}

Extend \eqref{eq:S-def} from $P_\lambda(V)$ to $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$ along
the second edge of $\mathcal R_V$. Both sides of
\eqref{eq:reference-comparison} are continuous along it, so the
comparison persists at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:large-length-counts}
For $\mathsf R$ and $\lambda$ sufficiently large,
\[
 N^{(\mathsf R)}_a=N_{a,\infty}\ (a=\pm),\qquad
 F^S_\lambda=N_{-,\infty}\otimes_RN_{+,\infty},\qquad
 \mathcal K_RF^Z_\lambda=(-1)^t\,N_{-,\infty}\otimes_RN_{+,\infty}.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Let $V$ be a completed index-zero solution on a separate side. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:separate-completion} with $\mathfrak F=\{V\}$, the
orientation $\Gamma^a$ of $W_{a,\mathsf R}$ at $P_{\mathsf R}(V)$ is the
continuous extension of $\Gamma^a_V$; along the second edge of
$\mathcal R_V$ it is continuous on the fixed domain. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-path} the canonical positive element is also
transported continuously from $V$ to $\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}(V)$. Hence $\Gamma^a(i\otimes1)$ has the
same sign at $\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}(V)$ as at $V$. The bijection
$\mathcal G_{\mathsf R}$ preserves labels and lifting orbits, so the
counts agree entry by entry.

In the joined case $S$ at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$ is, by definition, the
continuous extension of \eqref{eq:S-infty} over $\mathcal R_V$, and the
same lemma transports the positive element. At index zero
$\ind D_{V_-}=0$, so \eqref{eq:S-infty} evaluated on the positive
element is the product of the two separate signs. The joined lifting
group is $\Phi_-\oplus\Phi_+$ and the bridge has no gluing parameter
(proof of Paper~I, Theorem~10.31), so the orbit counts multiply. The generator
factors in \eqref{eq:S-infty} are those of the right tensor of maps
\eqref{eq:tensor-maps} of the even maps $N_{\pm,\infty}$. This proves
the second identity. The third follows from the second and
\eqref{eq:KR-G} with $m=0$, applied solution by solution, since
\eqref{eq:reference-comparison} holds at $\mathcal G_\lambda(V)$.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Boundary orientation of the comparison families}
\label{subsec:torus-families}
The comparison families below are those of Paper~I:
Lemma~10.15 with Propositions~10.16 and~10.17 for collar length and
width, Lemma~10.20 for the change of data at fixed width,
Theorem~5.4 for compactly supported changes of secondary data, and the
mixed-end family in the proof of Lemma~9.7, which produces $H_M$ in
Proposition~\ref{prop:closed-neck}. In each
case the data are constant near $b=0,1$, the endpoints are regular in
index at most one, and a relative invariant regularization gives:
finitely many isolated universal solutions of vertical index $-1$, each
with invertible augmented operator $(v,\tau)\mapsto Dv+\tau C$, and a
compact one-manifold of universal index-zero solutions whose boundary
consists of the two endpoint fibres and one branch for each pair of an
index $-1$ solution with an index-one trajectory at an ordinary end.
Here $C$ is the parameter column; on a varying mixed end it is the
weighted column of Paper~I, Lemma~10.15, not a compactly supported one.

Let $\Omega$ denote the orientation map in use: $\Gamma^a$ on a
separate domain, $\Gamma^M$ on the closed-cut piece, or $G$ on a joined
domain. Put $f=0$ in the first two cases and $f=t$ in the third. The
endpoint counts $F_0,F_1$ are oriented by $\Omega$ on the positive
element. At an isolated universal solution $c$, orient the parameter
first: choose $h\in\det D_c$ such that $db\otimes h$ is the positive
element of the invertible augmented operator, and count $c$ by
$\Omega(i\otimes h)$, or $\Omega(i\otimes q\otimes h)$ when $f=t$. Let
$H$ be the resulting count. Its degree is $f+1$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:family-signs}
With these conventions,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:family-homotopy}
 F_1-F_0=d_{\rm out}H+(-1)^fHd_{\rm in}=\delta H .
\end{equation}
For a fixed regular datum, the index-one boundary gives
$d_{\rm out}F=(-1)^fFd_{\rm in}$, so $F$ is closed of degree $f$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Orient the universal index-zero space by $\det(T_b[0,1])\otimes\det D$
and $\Omega$, as in Section~\ref{sec:determinants}; the boundary is
oriented by the outward normal first. At $b=1$ the outward normal is
$\partial_b$, and at $b=0$ it is $-\partial_b$; there the vertical
operators are invertible, so these contribute $F_1-F_0$.

At an outgoing strip break $(c,u)$ the glued solutions are
$U_T=\Psi(p_T+z_T)$, where the preglue $p_T$ has the strip translated
to $u(s-T,\cdot)$ and $z_T$ is the small correction of Paper~I,
Theorem~5.1. Differentiate the defining identity of $z_T$ in $T$,
using local identifications of the domains which fix the mixed core
and translate the strip core. The $T$-derivative of $p_T$ is the
cut-off translation vector $-\partial_su$ plus an error which is
exponentially small in $T$. By the uniform right inverse, the
$T$-derivative of $z_T$ is bounded by the $T$-derivative of the
residual of $p_T$ and by $\|z_T\|$ times a bounded quantity, and both
tend to zero. For pseudoholomorphic curves this $T$-derivative estimate,
with exponential decay, is \cite[Theorem~6.4]{FOOO-decay}; the same
argument applies on the fixed ordinary gauge end.
Hence $\partial_TU_T=-\partial_su+o(1)$ on the strip core after
projection to the slice. Linear gluing (Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing})
identifies $\det A_c\otimes\det D_u$ with the determinant along the
branch, and the positive translation vector $\partial_su$ orients
$\det D_u$. The outward direction at $T=\infty$ is $\partial_T$, which
is negative in this order. With the parameter already first in
$\det A_c=\det(T_b[0,1])\otimes\det D_c$ and
\eqref{eq:strip-coherence} or Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(ii), this
branch contributes $-d_{\rm out}H$.

At an incoming break $(A,c)$, with $A$ an index-one trajectory, the
trajectory is translated to $A(s+T,\cdot)$, and the same argument gives
$\partial_TU_T=+\partial_sA+o(1)$, positive in
$\det D_A\otimes\det A_c$. The outward direction is again $\partial_T$.
Moving the one-dimensional parameter line to the front past the odd
line $\det D_A$ contributes $-1$. The input compatibility of $\Omega$
(Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} for $\Gamma$,
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(i) for $G$) contributes $(-1)^{f}$,
because $\ind D_A=1$. This branch contributes $-(-1)^fHd_{\rm in}$.
For a joined domain with two inputs, the other-arm permutations are
those of the right tensor differential, by
Lemma~\ref{lem:tensor-geometric}. The total signed boundary vanishes,
which is \eqref{eq:family-homotopy}. Every branch is counted once with
its matched lifting orbit: Paper~I, Theorem~5.4(ii), gives one branch
per pair, and Theorem~5.1(ii),(iv) with hypothesis (H5) there gives the
orbit matching.

For a fixed datum the same computation without the parameter line
gives $-d_{\rm out}F+(-1)^fFd_{\rm in}=0$. The sign determinations use
only the direction of the translation vector, the ordinary linear
gluing of Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} at the ordinary end where the
break occurs, and the invertibility of the bordered augmented operator
along the gluing paths; no orientation is attached to a physical
kernel at a nonsolution. By Corollary~\ref{cor:strip-actions} the
output differential is the same at every point of the family.
\end{proof}

For a simple check of the incoming sign, take $D_A:\RR v\to0$,
$D_c:0\to\RR y$ and $C:\RR\mu\to\RR y$ the identity. In the order
$\det D_A\otimes\det A_c$ the kernel is oriented by $v$. In the
parameter-first order $\mu\otimes v\otimes y^*$ cancelling $\mu$
against $y$ leaves $-v$.

\subsection{The finite families and the closed neck}
\label{subsec:torus-finite}
\begin{proposition}\label{prop:finite-families}
Let $N_\pm$ be the regular vertex maps at $\mathsf R=0$, and fix a
regular joined datum at a width $\lambda_0>0$. Choose $\mathsf R$ and
$\Lambda$ as in Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts}, and at these
lengths add small invariant secondary perturbations supported in the
cores which make index one regular and preserve the index-zero
counts. Then there are maps $P_\pm$ of degree one and $B_J$ of degree
$t+1$ with
\[
 N_{a,\infty}-N_a=\delta P_a,\qquad
 F^Z_\Lambda-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B_J .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The added perturbations exist by Paper~I, Section~10.11, step~(5): the
index-zero sets are finite and regular, so openness and compactness
preserve them, their signs and the absence of negative indices. For the
separate side, join the data at $\mathsf R=0$ to those at the large
length by one family which first changes the collar length and then
makes a compactly supported change of secondary data. Its endpoints are
regular through index one. Paper~I, Lemma~10.15, Proposition~10.16 and
Theorem~5.4, give its relative regularization and compactification,
applied to the concatenated family, whose two segments each satisfy
the hypotheses of Lemma~10.15, and Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with
$\Omega=\Gamma^a$ gives $N^{(\mathsf R)}_a-N_a=\delta P_a$. Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts}
replaces $N^{(\mathsf R)}_a$ by $N_{a,\infty}$. For the joined side use
one family consisting of the width change of Paper~I,
Proposition~10.17, from $\lambda_0$ to $\Lambda$, followed by the change
of secondary data at width $\Lambda$, regularize it relatively as a
single family, and apply Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with
$\Omega=G$. The joined families lie
at finite widths throughout, so $G$ is defined along them.
\end{proof}

With the right tensor convention,
$\delta(F\otimes_RG)=(-1)^{|G|}\delta F\otimes_RG+F\otimes_R\delta G$.
Since $N_\pm$ and $N_{\pm,\infty}$ are even and closed,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:P-homotopy}
 P=P_-\otimes_RN_++N_{-,\infty}\otimes_RP_+,\qquad
 \delta P=N_{-,\infty}\otimes_RN_{+,\infty}-N_-\otimes_RN_+ .
\end{equation}
By Proposition~\ref{prop:KR-closed} and \eqref{eq:compose-homotopy},
$\delta(\mathcal K_RB_J)=(-1)^t\mathcal K_R\delta B_J$. Together with
Proposition~\ref{prop:large-length-counts} this gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:width-homotopy}
 (-1)^t\mathcal K_RF^Z_{\lambda_0}-N_-\otimes_RN_+
 =\delta\bigl(P-\mathcal K_RB_J\bigr).
\end{equation}

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:closed-neck}
Let $N_M$ be the count on the closed-cut piece $M_{\lambda_0}$, with the
data of $N_Z$ at width $\lambda_0$, oriented by $\Gamma^M$. There are
maps $B_{\rm cut}$ of degree $t+1$ and $H_M$ of degree one with
\[
 N_M\overline{\mathsf E}-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B_{\rm cut},\qquad
 N_Z-N_M=\delta H_M .
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Paper~I, Proposition~10.21, inserts a closed neck $[-\ell,\ell]\times Z$
carrying the cut-off term $f_Z$, and shows that for large $\ell$, after
a small invariant perturbation making index one regular, the joined
index-zero solutions are exactly the gluings $A\#_Zc'$ of index-zero
solutions on $\overline{\mathcal E}$ and on $M_{\lambda_0}$, one for each matched
lifting orbit. The lifting group is the fibre product over $\phi_Z$
and all three pieces are connected, so by
Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity} the gluing fibre is trivial and the
quotient count is the matrix composite. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties}(iii), at index zero the orientation $G$ of
$A\#_Zc'$ is $\Gamma^M_{c'}$ applied to the right-placed conversion of
$\gamma_A$; the prefactor $(-1)^{t\ind D_A}$ is one. Linear gluing along
the long neck is continuous and the operators along the gluing paths
are invertible, so the sign is that of the composite. Hence the count
at the long-neck data is $N_M\overline{\mathsf E}$. Paper~I,
Lemma~10.20, connects the data at width $\lambda_0$ to the long-neck
data by a family with compactly supported parameter derivative, and
Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs} with $\Omega=G$ gives $B_{\rm cut}$.

For the second identity, the mixed-end family in the proof of Paper~I,
Lemma~9.7, as applied in Paper~I, Proposition~10.21, changes the finite
torus-neck lengths of
$D_{i,\lambda_0}$ to those of the special quintuple of $(D_0,D_1)$,
together with a compact interpolation of the metric, with the ordinary
datum on $Z$ fixed. This is a noncollapsing family with the weighted
parameter column of Paper~I, Lemma~10.15. Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs}
with $\Omega=\Gamma^M$ gives $H_M$. The input complex $C_G(Z)$ is the
same at both ends, since $f_Z$ is fixed.
\end{proof}

Since $\overline{\mathsf E}$, with the conversion at $Z$, is closed,
$\delta(H_M\overline{\mathsf E})=(N_Z-N_M)\overline{\mathsf E}$ by
\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}. With
$B=B_{\rm cut}+H_M\overline{\mathsf E}$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:neck-homotopy}
 N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}-F^Z_{\lambda_0}=\delta B .
\end{equation}

\subsection{The mixed excision identity}
\label{subsec:mixed-excision}
\begin{theorem}[Mixed excision]\label{thm:mixed-excision}
Assume the unsigned analytic results of
Paper~I. With the conversion at the closed cut placed as in
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line} and $\mathcal K_R$ as in
\eqref{eq:KR-def},
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-explicit}
 (-1)^t\mathcal K_RN_Z\overline{\mathsf E}-N_-\otimes_RN_+=\delta H,
 \qquad
 H=P-\mathcal K_RB_J+\mathcal K_RB .
\end{equation}
Each term of $H$ is a signed count of isolated universal solutions in
an actual one-parameter family, or its composite or right tensor with
closed counts. Here $\overline{\mathsf E}$ is the reverse excision map at the
closed-cut data of Proposition~\ref{prop:closed-neck}; by
Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(c) it is chain homotopic to the map at
the data of that proposition. In particular
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-reverse}
 \mathcal K_RN_Z\overline{\mathsf E}\simeq(-1)^t(N_-\otimes_RN_+).
\end{equation}
For $z\in C_G(Z)$ in the structure of $\overline{\mathcal E}$, write
$N_Zz$ for $N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}z$, as in the composite
$N_Z\overline{\mathsf E}$. Then for a homogeneous cycle $z$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:excision-cycle}
 [\mathcal K_RN_Zz]=\pm[(N_-\otimes_RN_+)\mathsf Ez].
\end{equation}
All terms are expressed at the fixed end bases.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By \eqref{eq:compose-homotopy} and Proposition~\ref{prop:KR-closed},
$\delta(\mathcal K_RB)=(-1)^t\mathcal K_R\delta B$. Adding this, applied
to \eqref{eq:neck-homotopy}, to \eqref{eq:width-homotopy} gives
\eqref{eq:excision-explicit}, and \eqref{eq:excision-reverse} is its
restatement. For \eqref{eq:excision-cycle} apply
\eqref{eq:excision-reverse} to $\mathsf Ez$. After the continuation
homotopy of Proposition~\ref{prop:selfgluing}(c), the capped comparison
gives $[\overline{\mathsf E}\mathsf Ez]=\pm[z]$ for this homogeneous
class, with one sign (Section~\ref{subsec:capped}), and applying
$\mathcal K_RN_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}$ gives the claim.
\end{proof}

The sign $(-1)^t$ in \eqref{eq:excision-reverse} refers to the fixed
choices of ordinary orientations, of the base orientation of $Q$ and of
the reference comparison \eqref{eq:K0}. Changing one of these choices
changes the identity by one overall sign; no choice changes individual
matrix entries.

Equality~\eqref{eq:excision-cycle} is equality of homology classes of
tensors. If the tensors differ by a boundary, applying the chain
isomorphism~\eqref{eq:beta} produces a boundary in the Hom complex.
This is the mechanism that will turn the handle-state calculation into
a chain homotopy. No inference from equality of induced maps on
homology is involved.

\section{The compression cup before reflection}\label{sec:cup}

\subsection{The tensor supplied by the folded triangle}
We spell out the geometry from Paper~I, Section~10.1. Write a
presentation $p$ of $M$ as a pair of torus compression bodies
$U_0,U_1$ of genus $h\ge2$, with perturbed Lagrangians
$\widetilde P_0,\widetilde P_1\subset\Msf_h$. Let $C=C_{h,g}$,
$g=h+k$, add $k$ handles and put $V_i=C\circ U_i$. The resulting
three-manifold is $M^+=M\#^k(S^1\times S^2)$. Restriction of flat
connections on $C$ gives the embedded Lagrangian correspondence
$L_C\subset\Msf_h^-\times\Msf_g$.

Choose product Morse height functions on the clean auxiliary
intersections
$L(V_i)\cap(L(U_i)\circ L_C)\cong\SU(2)^{g-1}$.
Each height function has $2^{g-1}$ critical points. Perturb the
primary data of $V_i$ by $t$ times this function, for $t>0$ small,
and write $R_i\subset\Msf_g$ for the resulting Lagrangian.
An \emph{upper height presentation} is the presentation with
compression bodies $V_0,V_1$, Lagrangians $R_0,R_1$, and these
height and auxiliary regular data. For this fixed upper height choose an open neighbourhood
$\mathcal N(t)$ of zero in the product of the lower primary
parameter spaces for $U_0,U_1$. Require the two auxiliary
height intersections to remain transverse, with their critical
points continuing uniquely from the product Morse model. Such a
neighbourhood exists by openness of transversality for these
compact intersections. Shrink it inside the chosen primary ball
and choose the lower data there; the additional generic conditions
on the lower pair are imposed below. This is the lower-data
neighbourhood used in Section~\ref{sec:choices}.
In the product symplectic manifold
\[
 X=\Msf_h^-\times\Msf_g,\qquad
 A_i=\widetilde P_i^-\times R_i,
\]
require the three pairs among $(A_0,L_C,A_1)$ to be transverse
and their triple intersection to be empty. These are the generic
conditions used for the folded triangle in Paper~I. The two
auxiliary complexes are $\CF_X(A_0,L_C)$ and
$\CF_X(L_C,A_1)$. Their distinguished top cycles are $q_0^*$ and
$q_1^*$, respectively, with top degree defined by the auxiliary integer grading $m$
of Paper~I, Section~11.3. Its differences satisfy
$m(a)-m(x)=\ind(u)-8\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}(u)$.
Here $\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}$ is the relative class of Paper~I's
auxiliary pair. Let $\mathcal A_i$ denote its critical action,
the values of the actual local exact-graph primitive, with the
sign fixed by the ordered pair. Writing $\omega_X$ for the
symplectic form of $X$, the relation is
\[
4\pi^2\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}(u)
 =\int u^*\omega_X-\bigl(\mathcal A_i(a)-\mathcal A_i(x)\bigr).
\]
At the flat height data $\mathcal A_i$ is the signed height
function $t h_i$; under small primary changes its actual values
are retained. Thus the symplectic area includes an endpoint correction;
its quotient by $4\pi^2$ is the total topological energy.
Attaching a positive generator sphere increases
$\kappa_{\mathrm{aux}}$ by $1/2$ and the index by $4$, leaving
$m(a)-m(x)$ unchanged. The normalization of $m$ agrees with
the Morse grading. The parity grading alone does not define a top
class. Depending on the order of the pair, the corresponding
critical point is the maximum or minimum of the height function.

Denote the chosen upper presentation by $q=C\circ p$; this notation
includes its height perturbation. With a split almost-complex
structure on $X$, folding identifies the output complex with
$\CF(\bar p)\otimes_R\CF(q)$. Let $\mathcal K_R$ be the closed
identification \eqref{eq:KR-def} of
Section~\ref{sec:excision}, with its transported orientation lines. It uses
the orientation system transported independently at $\bar p$; the reflection pairing
with $\CF(p)^\vee$ will be constructed later. The triangle product
for the ordered triple $(A_0,L_C,A_1)$, including the conversion
 $\Theta$ at its closed gauge cut $Z$ in the position of
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}, is denoted by $\mu_X^\Theta$.
It gives the cycle
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cup-tensor}
 z_C=\mathcal K_R\mu_X^\Theta(q_0^*\otimes_R q_1^*)
       \in\CF(\bar p)\otimes_R\CF(q).
\end{equation}
Define
\begin{equation}\label{eq:check-cup}
 \check U_C=B_{\CF(\bar p)}(z_C):
       \CF(\bar p)^\vee\longrightarrow\CF(q).
\end{equation}
For $k=0$, $L_C$ is the diagonal and we denote the upper height
presentation by $r$. An \emph{admissible fold pair} $(p,r)$ means
such a lower and upper presentation in one common admissible ball,
with a height $t$, lower data in $\mathcal N(t)$, and the
transversality and auxiliary regularity conditions just stated.
Put $B(p,r)=\check U_C$ for this pair. Thus a fold pair is a
particular pair of nearby presentations with a fixed diagonal
triangle and its two height caps.

The top cycles are normalized using the integral
mixed maps on the auxiliary vertices:
$q_i^*=\pm N_i\theta_i$. The sign is one sign
of each distinguished cycle. The auxiliary
complexes have the standard filtration. The
unit-diagonal calculation in
Proposition~\ref{prop:N-isomorphism} gives the
coefficient $\pm1$ on $q_i^*$, and the other
coefficients vanish by the grading argument of
Paper~I, Lemma~9.2 and Proposition~9.4, with
Lemma~11.7 for the folded pairs, which uses no
signs. This is an integral normalization, not
merely a mod-two one.

\subsection{The gauge handle state}
Let $B_k$ be the gauge filling whose closed
boundary is $Z$, obtained from the folded
triangle and its two auxiliary fillings. Its
relative invariant is a homogeneous cycle
$\Theta_{B_k}\in C_G(Z)$. Choose a single
almost-complex structure on this filling and
use its restrictions on the pieces. Convert
at $Z$ to the structure used for mixed torus
excision. After excision the resulting state
is a cycle on
$C_G(-M)\otimes_R C_G(M^+)$. It is the state of
the bent cobordism $W_k^\#$: by Paper~I,
Lemma~11.1(ii), $B_k\cup_Z\mathcal E\cong W_k^\#$
with the incoming end read as outgoing and with
lifting group $\phi_{W_k}$. Gluing at the connected
cut $Z$ has no extra gluing fibre
(Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity}), and the orbit
count~\eqref{eq:orbit-count} gives the integral
coefficients.

Bending the source end of the one-handle
cobordism $W_k$ gives the identity
\begin{equation}\label{eq:handle-state}
 (\Phi_{G,M}\otimes_R1)[\mathsf E\Theta_{B_k}]
       =\pm[\ten(\IG(W_k))].
\end{equation}
To check the sign convention, let $\kappa$ be the
state of the product cylinder with both ends bent
outgoing. Gluing the doubly incoming cylinder of
Section~\ref{sec:gauge} to it along $-M$ gives the
product cylinder. In ordered determinant gluing the
line of $x\in C_G(M)$ passes the $-M$ factor of
$\kappa=\sum\kappa_1\otimes\kappa_2$, so the glued map is
$x\mapsto\sum(-1)^{|x||\kappa_1|}\langle\kappa_1,x\rangle_M\kappa_2$,
and it is $\pm1$. By the definition of $\Phi_{G,M}$,
$\langle y,x\rangle_M=\ev'(\Phi_{G,M}y\otimes x)$, where
$\ev'(f\otimes x)=(-1)^{|f|}f(x)$ is the closed
evaluation for~\eqref{eq:tensor}. The same composite
formed with $\ev'$ and $\ten(1)$ is the identity, so
$(\Phi_{G,M}\otimes1)\kappa=\pm\ten(1)$, with one sign. Glue $W_k$ to its second
end and use the ordered determinant gluing.
This gives the tensor of the right-placed
map. An odd endpoint conversion is already
part of that map through
\eqref{eq:theta-grading}; moving it to the
other side would change the tensor by a
grading operator. The residual signs in
\eqref{eq:handle-state} are therefore scalar.
The same argument holds for $k=0$, when the
cobordism map is gauge continuation.

\subsection{The cup formula}
Define the transposed reflected mixed map
\begin{equation}\label{eq:n-p}
 n_p=\ev_{C_G(M)}^{-1}
       (N_{\bar p}\Phi_{G,M}^{-1})^\vee:
       \CF(\bar p)^\vee\longrightarrow C_G(M).
\end{equation}
It is an even chain isomorphism, using the
paired choices of gauge base. This definition
uses gauge duality and the mixed map at the
reflected presentation; it makes no assumption
about symplectic duality.

The integral version of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8, is the folded form of
the triangle comparison \eqref{eq:triangle-square}.

\begin{lemma}[Folded triangle comparison]\label{lem:folded-triangle}
Let $N_0$, $N_1$ be the integral mixed maps of the product-target pairs
$(A_0,L_C)$ and $(L_C,A_1)$, with gauge inputs $Z_0=D_0\cup(-C)$ and
$Z_1=C\cup(-D_1)$, and let $\mu_{\mathscr W}:C_G(Z_0)\otimes_R C_G(Z_1)\to
C_G(Z)$ be the integral gauge map of the cobordism formed by the pieces
$D_0$, $C$, $D_1$. With the conversion at $Z$ placed as in
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line},
\[
 \mu_X^\Theta(N_0\otimes_RN_1)\simeq\pm N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\mu_{\mathscr W}.
\]
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The unsigned argument is the proof of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8: the family
of data of Paper~I, Lemma~9.12, collapses the middle compression body
$C$, which is an odd compression body with Lagrangian $L_C$, stretches the
two ordinary necks cutting off the vertex domains of $(A_0,L_C)$ and
$(L_C,A_1)$, and stretches the closed cut $Z$. We orient every
configuration in the family by the several-input construction of
Section~\ref{sec:determinants}, with the conversion line at $Z$ in its
fixed position. At the collapsed end, Theorem~\ref{thm:B} identifies the
orientations of the index-zero solutions with the orientations of the
symplectic triangle glued to the two mixed vertices. It applies to this
collapse: Section~\ref{sec:collapse} rests on the collapse analysis of
Paper~I, Sections~6--8, and the proof of Paper~I, Theorem~11.8, supplies its
inputs for the collapse of $C=C_{h,g}$, with product target, both boundary surfaces
matched and the lower sheet reversed, using the two-trace slice space
$H^1(\Sigma_h;\ad)\oplus H^1(\Sigma_g;\ad)$. The sign comparison of
Proposition~\ref{prop:collapse-sign} uses only canonical orientations
along paths of invertible operators, so the reversed sheet does not
enter it. Ordinary gluing along
the two stretched necks (Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing}) gives the count
$\mu_X^\Theta(N_0\otimes_RN_1)$, with the input lines in cyclic order. At
the stretched cut, gluing at the connected closed cut $Z$ has no extra
gluing fibre (Lemma~\ref{lem:gluing-multiplicity}). The stretched cut
decomposes the domain into the triangle cobordism of $D_0$, $C$, $D_1$,
glued at $Z$ to the closed-cut vertex. Lemma~\ref{lem:G-properties} is
stated for $W^J_\lambda$, but its proof uses only a decomposition at a
closed cut, the localized shift, which
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(b) supplies on folded triangles, and
Convention~\ref{conv:interior-line}. The same proof gives parts (i)--(iii)
for this decomposition, and part (iii) identifies the orientation with that
of the gauge cobordism followed by the conversion and the mixed map; this
gives $N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\mu_{\mathscr W}$. Parts (i) and (ii) give the
compatibility at the input and output breaks. The family between the two
ends is a compact one-parameter family with compactly supported parameter
derivative. Its boundary strata are the two endpoints and ordinary breaks
at the two gauge inputs, ordered by Lemma~\ref{lem:tensor-geometric} as in
the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs}, and at the output strip; the
collapse-scale stage is a family of the same kind. Lemma~\ref{lem:family-signs},
with $f=t$, turns its compactified index-zero space into the stated
homotopy. The overall sign
reflects the fixed orientation choices at the two ends.
\end{proof}

\begin{proposition}[Cup formula]\label{prop:cup-formula}
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cup-formula}
 \check U_C\simeq\pm N_q\IG(W_k)n_p.
\end{equation}
In particular, for a lower datum $p$ and a
compatible upper height datum $r$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:cup-zero}
 B(p,r)\simeq\pm N_r\cont_G(p,r)n_p.
\end{equation}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Apply Lemma~\ref{lem:folded-triangle} to
the two distinguished input cycles
$q_i^*=\pm N_i\theta_i$. Since the gauge
filling $B_k$ is the triangle cobordism
glued to the two auxiliary fillings,
$\Theta_{B_k}$ is homologous to
$\pm\mu_{\mathscr W}(\theta_0\otimes\theta_1)$
by gauge composition. Hence the
folded triangle output is, up to a
boundary and one sign,
$N_Z\Theta^{\mathrm r}\Theta_{B_k}$. Apply
\eqref{eq:excision-cycle} to the homogeneous
cycle $\Theta_{B_k}$. The result is
\[
 [z_C]=\pm[(N_{\bar p}\otimes_R N_q)
                          \mathsf E\Theta_{B_k}].
\]
Equation~\eqref{eq:handle-state} identifies
this with the tensor of $N_q\IG(W_k)n_p$.
The identification follows directly from
\eqref{eq:beta} and the graded double-dual
map in~\eqref{eq:n-p}. Since the difference
of the tensors is a boundary, their images
under $B_{\CF(\bar p)}$ differ by $\delta K$.
This proves~\eqref{eq:cup-formula}. The same
calculation with $k=0$ gives
\eqref{eq:cup-zero}.
\end{proof}

This proves a formula for the cup tensor
without selecting the reflection pairing
needed for the source of Paper~I's map.
The next sections construct that pairing
and show that it gives the ordinary
symplectic continuation when $k=0$.

\section{Sphere characters and variation of primary data}
\label{sec:characters}

\subsection{The character of an orientation system}
For a transverse pair $P=(P_0,P_1)$ let
$\mathcal P(P_0,P_1)$ be the path space of paths in
$\Msf_h$ starting on $P_0$ and ending on $P_1$.
For $h\ge2$, the compression-body Lagrangians are
simply connected and have vanishing second homotopy
group. The relative homotopy classes of strip
configurations with fixed ends are consequently a
torsor for $\pi_2(\Msf_h)\cong\ZZ$ in the lift
convention of~\cite[Section~2]{DFL}. Fix its positive
generator $S_h$; attaching it changes the index by
four. For $h=1$ the moduli space is a point and the
sphere-character assertions below are empty.

The determinant line of a sphere operator, together
with the inverse determinant of the matching tangent
space at its node, has its complex orientation.
Compare node gluing with the coherent strip
orientation $\Gamma^P$. The ratio is a sign
\begin{equation}\label{eq:sphere-character}
 \chi^P:\pi_2(\Msf_h)\longrightarrow\{1,-1\}.
\end{equation}
Associativity of node gluing makes it a character.
Multiplying generator lines by signs does not change
it: the two endpoint factors occur in both sides of
the comparison. Multiplying them by their grading
signs has the same property. Thus the character is
information about the configuration-level coherent
system, beyond the signs of its differential.

At a transverse point $x$, a second description is
available. Attach a fixed representative sphere to
the constant mixed pair $C_x$ and compare its
transported mixed orientation with the product of
the canonical orientation of $C_x$ and the complex
node orientation. Denote the ratio by
$\chi(h,x;d)$, where $h$ is the primary datum and
$d$ contains the regularizing and almost-complex
data.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:pointed-character}
Whenever $x$ is a transverse intersection point,
$\chi(h,x;d)=\chi^P(S_h)$. The ratio is constant along
any compact smooth family of primary data carrying
a persistent transverse intersection point. It is
also independent of small regularizing data at
fixed primary data and fixed gauge base.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Glue a strip to the constant pair and attach the
sphere away from the ordinary gluing collar; moving
the sphere from the constant pair to the strip keeps
the node in the interior of a pure strip, where
Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}(c) applies. The
two possible orders of these operations induce
the same determinant-line isomorphism. The sphere
and node factors are complex, hence even as real
graded lines, so commuting them with the strip
factor gives no sign. Cancelling the strip
orientation gives equality of the pointed and
strip definitions. A strip configuration exists
between any two intersection points, in some
relative homotopy class; it need not solve the
holomorphic equation. Applying the same argument
to that configuration shows independence of $x$.

For a family with a persistent point, the
constant-pair operators and the node-gluing map vary
continuously by Proposition~\ref{prop:foundations}, and the
transported orientation of Proposition~\ref{prop:transport} by
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}.
The ratio is a continuous function to
$\{1,-1\}$ and is therefore constant. To vary
regularizing data, use the segment to zero data.
The perturbations are supported off the strip
end, and the constant-pair operators stay
invertible. Transporting their canonical
orientations along the segment proves the last
assertion. Compatible almost-complex structures
can be varied through the same argument, with
the gauge base held fixed.
\end{proof}

The persistent point need only remain transverse
at that point. Other intersections of the two
Lagrangians may become degenerate. The lemma is
about determinant lines over configurations and
therefore does not require a regular moduli
space along the family.

\subsection{A common ball of primary perturbations}
Let $\mathcal C_i$ be the finite real span of the
sign-sensitive holonomy functions on $C_i^\circ$,
modulo constants. Use the norm
\begin{align}
 \|h_i\|_i={}&\sup_B\|\nabla_Bh_i\|_{L^\infty}
  +\sup_{B\ne B'}
   \frac{\|\nabla_Bh_i-\nabla_{B'}h_i\|_{L^2}}
        {\|B-B'\|_{L^2}},\label{eq:primary-norm}\\
 \|h\|={}&\max(\|h_0\|_0,\|h_1\|_1).\notag
\end{align}
The suprema are taken over smooth connections in a
fixed determinant bundle. Cylinder functions have
finite norm by the holonomy estimates of
\cite[Proposition~6.1]{DFL}. If the norm vanishes,
the gradient vanishes on the affine connection
space, so the function is constant; thus this is
a norm modulo constants.

Choose a finite trace family $F_0$ whose restrictions
to the compact flat-connection moduli spaces give
embedding coordinates. In the model
$L(C_i)\cong\SU(2)^{h-1}$, quaternion coordinates
can be obtained from sign-sensitive traces using
the fixed holonomies on the odd torus. Such traces
distinguish central sign changes; passing to
unsigned $\SO(3)$ traces would lose that property.
For a finite family $F$ containing $F_0$, put
\[
 \mathcal U(F)=\{h\in\operatorname{span}F:\|h\|<r_*\},
 \qquad \mathfrak B=\bigcup_F\mathcal U(F).
\]
The radius is independent of the additional
functions in $F$, for the fixed splitting and
metric data. These sets are nested under
inclusion of finite families and are convex.

\begin{proposition}\label{prop:small-ball}
The radius $r_*>0$ can be chosen so that every
$h$ in this ball has the following properties.
\begin{enumerate}[label=(\roman*)]
\item The perturbed flat moduli space of each
compression body is a smooth compact manifold
identified with its unperturbed moduli space;
its boundary restriction is an embedded
Lagrangian close to the unperturbed one.
\item The connection representatives and their
first derivatives in the moduli parameters
are close in $L^2_1$ and $L^2$, respectively,
uniformly as $\|h\|\to0$.
\item The functions from $F_0$ remain embedding
coordinates on these perturbed moduli spaces.
\item If the two boundary Lagrangians intersect
transversely, the corresponding closed gauge
critical points are nondegenerate and are
exactly the lifts of those intersections.
\end{enumerate}
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The norm gives
$\|F_B\|_{L^\infty}\le\|h_i\|_i$ for an
$h_i$-flat connection. If a sequence with norm
tending to zero escaped every gauge neighborhood
of the flat moduli space, Uhlenbeck compactness
on the compact compression body would give a
subsequence weakly converging in $W^{1,4}$,
after gauge, to a flat connection. The
$W^{1,4}$ embedding in $C^0$ is compact in
three dimensions. A Coulomb--Neumann slice
relative to the flat limit and the elliptic
estimate for $(d_B^*,d_B)$ then improve this
to convergence in $L^2_1$, a contradiction.
The boundary slice and compactness statements
are the versions for a manifold with boundary
in~\cite{Wehrheim-book}.

In a slice at a flat connection split into the
finite-dimensional harmonic space and its
complement. The linearized perturbed-flat
operator on the complement has a bounded
right inverse. The second term of
\eqref{eq:primary-norm} makes the holonomy
part a small Lipschitz perturbation in
$L^2$, so the contraction gives a graph over
the harmonic space. Differentiating its
fixed-point equation in the finite-dimensional
parameter gives the asserted first-derivative
control. Hodge decomposition and boundary
elliptic regularity identify these solutions
with the smooth perturbed-flat solutions;
the exact regularity inputs are recorded in
Appendix~\ref{app:inputs}. Compactness of the
flat moduli space makes the thresholds
uniform over finitely many slice charts.

For a trace function $\tau$, its bounded
Lipschitz gradient extends the identity
\[
 \tau(B')-\tau(B)=\int_0^1
 \langle\nabla_{B+s(B'-B)}\tau,B'-B\rangle\,ds
\]
to $L^2$ connections. Differentiating on the
graph family shows that the trace-coordinate
map remains $C^1$-close to its unperturbed
embedding. Openness of embeddings of a
compact manifold proves (iii). The matching
exact sequence for the two compression-body
boundary problems identifies the kernel
of the closed Hessian with the tangent
intersection of the Lagrangians, using the
restriction and deformation sequences of
\cite[Propositions~2.10 and~2.16]{DFL}; hence
transversality gives (iv).
\end{proof}

No radius controlling all higher derivatives
of every cylinder function is asserted here.
