data: perturbed flatness involves no metric, and the weights are
functions of the end coordinates. Each one-input domain of
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(a) with finite torus length is identified
with a special quintuple by a diffeomorphism of decorated manifolds,
depending continuously on the lengths and widths: for $W_{a,\mathsf R}$
the map $\psi_{\mathsf R}$ of Paper~I, Definition~10.1(a), which is
independent of $\theta$ and is a translation near the flat face; for the
closed-cut piece with input $Z$ the identification of Paper~I,
Lemma~10.18(ii); for the vertex domains of Paper~I, Lemma~9.7, a
decorated diffeomorphism equal to the identity outside a compact set.
The domains of that lemma differ from the special quintuple by
compactly supported changes of the seam placement and of the neck
lengths (proof of Paper~I, Lemma~9.7), which the isotopy extension
theorem realizes by such a diffeomorphism; it carries end coordinates
to end coordinates, and the metric does not enter the configuration
space. The output regions used in part~(b) are identified in
Section~\ref{app:shift-local}. It therefore suffices to construct the
deformations on a special quintuple, for families in which the primary
data vary in a compact set.

\subsection{Preparation}\label{app:shift-prep}
Write $C_Y=[-2,0]_s\times\Sigma$ for the collar of $\Sigma=\partial Y$ in
$Y$ which forms part of $U_-\times\Sigma$ on the mixed ends
\cite[Section~3.1]{DFL}, and $C_{Y'}$ likewise. It extends to a flat
collar $[-3,0]\times\Sigma$ whose part $[-3,-2]\times\Sigma$ in $Y_0$ is free of
the primary perturbation, so that primary solutions are flat on it. The
collars of $\partial Y_0$ used below are $[-9/4,-2]\times\Sigma$, in the
normal form, and $[-5/2,-2]\times\Sigma$, in the definition of
$\mathcal T_x$. We use the following normal form, with constants
$\Theta\ge2$ and $S\ge3$:
\begin{enumerate}[label=(N\arabic*)]
\item $u$ is the constant map to $x$ on $[S,\infty)\times[-1,1]$;
\item near $\eta_+$ and $\eta'_+$, $u$ is independent of the normal
coordinate;
\item on the mixed-end regions $|\theta|\ge\Theta$ the configuration is a
stationary pair $(\pi^*B,q)$, $(\pi'^*B',q')$, where $B$ equals
$\mathrm{pr}^*b$ on $[-9/4,0]\times\Sigma$, that is, on $C_Y$ and on the collar of
width $1/4$ of $\partial Y_0$ in $Y_0$, for a flat connection $b$ on
$\Sigma$, and similarly for $B'$;
\item $u(s,\theta)$ is independent of $s$ for $s\in[0,1]$;
\item on $[-1,0]\times\RR_\theta\times\Sigma$ the connection is the pullback
of a connection $b(\theta)+\psi(\theta)\,d\theta$ on $\RR_\theta\times\Sigma$
whose slices $b(\theta)$ are flat, with $\psi=0$ for $|\theta|\ge\Theta$.
\end{enumerate}

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:preparation}
For a family $f:K\to\mathcal A(a,x)$ on the special quintuple there are
$\Theta,S$ and a deformation $f_t$, $t\in[0,1]$, jointly continuous in
$(k,t)$ and in the data, with $f_0=f$ and $f_1$ in normal form. It is
the identity on the gauge input end $(-\infty,-3]\times Y_\#$. Members
that are stationary on the original ends beyond $\Theta$ and $S$ remain
so, and members of $\mathcal T_x$ are fixed.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
\emph{Collar gauge at the mixed ends.} Let $B_q$ be the actual limit
representative at the $+$ mixed end; it is continuous in the family and
flat on the flat collar. Let $K$ be its temporal gauge along the collar,
the solution of $\partial_sK=-(B_q)_sK$ on $[-3,0]$ with $K(0)=1$. Then
$K^*B_q$ has no $ds$ component on $[-3,0]\times\Sigma$ and, being flat, is a
product there. Choose a smooth $\sigma_N:[-3,0]\to[-5/2,0]$ with
$\sigma_N(s)=s$ on $[-9/4,0]$ and $\sigma_N=0$ near $-3$, and put
$k_\lambda=K(\lambda\sigma_N(s))$, extended by $1$. This is a path of gauge
transformations from $1$ to $k=k_1$, with $k_\lambda=1$ at the seam for every
$\lambda$, and $k^*B_q=\mathrm{pr}^*b_q$ on $[-9/4,0]\times\Sigma$, because $k=K$
there. Apply the gauge $k_{\lambda(\theta)}$ on the $+$ mixed end, with
$\lambda(\theta)=0$ for $\theta\le\Theta_0$ and $1$ for $\theta\ge\Theta_0+1$,
and do the same at the $-$ end. Gauge transformations preserve the
matching condition, and this one is the identity at the seam. If the limit
representative is already a product on $[-5/2,0]\times\Sigma$, then $K=1$ on
$[-5/2,0]$, which contains the range of $\sigma_N$, so $k_\lambda=1$; in
particular members of $\mathcal T_x$ are fixed.

\emph{Ends.} On the strip output end write $u=\exp_x\xi$ in an exponential
chart at $x$ in which both Lagrangians are linear, and put
$u_t=\exp_x((1-t\beta)\xi)$ with a cutoff $\beta(s)$ equal to one for
$s\ge S$. On a mixed end, beyond some $\Theta_1\ge\Theta_0+1$ the
configuration lies in the matching chart $\Psi_{R_q}$ (Section~\ref{app:local})
centered at its stationary limit $R_q=(\pi^*k^*B_q,q)$:
$c=\Psi_{R_q}(\zeta)$ with $\zeta$ in the linearized boundary conditions.
Put $c_t=\Psi_{R_q}((1-t\beta(\theta))\zeta)$ with $\beta=1$ for
$\theta\ge\Theta$. The chart is fibrewise over the base, and the linear
conditions are pointwise in $\theta$, so they are preserved; the chart
gives the nonlinear matching and Lagrangian conditions. A compact family
has uniformly small tails in the topology of Section~\ref{sec:geometry},
so common $\Theta_1,\Theta,S$ exist, and the charts depend continuously on
the limits and the primary data. Members already stationary beyond $\Theta$
remain so.

\emph{Collars.} Let $\phi_0:[0,\infty)\to[0,\infty)$ be smooth and
nondecreasing, with $\phi_0=0$ on $[0,1]$ and $\phi_0(s)=s$ near and beyond
$2$, and put $\phi_t=(1-t)\mathrm{id}+t\phi_0$. Pull back $u$ by $\phi_t$ in the
normal coordinate of the seam in $U_+$, and $A$ by
$(s,\theta,y)\mapsto(-\phi_t(-s),\theta,y)$ on $[-2,0]\times\RR\times\Sigma$;
since $\phi_t$ is the identity near $2$, the result is smooth where
$[-2,0]\times\Sigma$ meets $Y_0$. Since $\phi_t(0)=0$, the seam values, hence
the matching condition, are unchanged. Do the same in the normal
coordinates of $\eta_+,\eta'_+$, which preserves the arc values. On the
strips $[0,2]\times[2,\infty)$ both maps act on the same coordinate and do
not commute. In the coordinate $s\in[0,2]$ across such a strip, with the
seam at $s=0$ and the arc at $s=2$, write
$\Phi_{\rm seam}(s)=\phi_t(s)$ and $\Phi_{\rm arc}(s)=2-\phi_t(2-s)$. Take
the arc pullback first and the seam pullback second, so that $u$ is
replaced by $u\circ\Phi_{\rm arc}\circ\Phi_{\rm seam}$. At $t=1$ this
composite maps $[0,1]$ to the seam, because $\phi_0=0$ there and
$\Phi_{\rm arc}(0)=0$, and a neighbourhood of $2$ to the arc, because
$\phi_0$ is the identity near $2$; for every $t$ it fixes the seam and
the arc pointwise. At $t=1$, (N2), (N4) and (N5) hold; stationarity
beyond $\Theta$ is preserved, and so is (N3), because $\mathrm{pr}^*b$ is
independent of $s$. Members of $\mathcal T_x$ are independent of $s$ on
these collars and are fixed.

All three steps are explicit, continuous in the data, and supported away
from the gauge input end.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Positive extension, shift and straightening}\label{app:shift-shift}
Use the reparametrizations $\Phi_\pm$ of \cite[Section~4.4]{DFL}, chosen
polar about the corners for $|\theta|\ge\Theta+1$, preserving the lines of
constant $\theta$ near the seam for $|\theta|\le\Theta$, smooth in between,
where the prepared configuration is stationary, and carrying the
normal collars of $\eta_+,\eta'_+$ onto collars of $t=\pm1$; DFL prescribe
$\Phi_\pm$ only on the ends. In these coordinates the gauge region is
$(-\infty,0]\times Y_\#$ with $\{0\}\times Y_0$ and $\{0\}\times Y_0'$
removed, the section domain is $[0,\infty)\times[-1,1]$ with the corners
$\pm i$ removed, the matching seam is $\{0\}\times[-1,1]\times\Sigma$ against
$\{0\}\times[-1,1]$, and the collars $C_Y,C_{Y'}$ of the mixed ends collapse
to $\{0\}\times\partial Y_0$ and $\{0\}\times\partial Y_0'$, the collar
coordinate becoming the angle about these corners. By (N3)--(N5) a prepared
configuration is independent of the normal coordinate near $r=0$, it is
$\mathrm{pr}^*b$ with no angular component near the corners, and its seam
restriction is $b(\theta(t))+\widetilde\psi(t)\,dt$ with
$\widetilde\psi(t)=\psi(\theta(t))\theta'(t)$, which vanishes near $t=\pm1$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:representatives}
Let $\mathcal P$ be the space of smooth primary solutions on $Y_0$ which
are products on the collar $[-9/4,-2]\times\Sigma$ of $\partial Y_0$, and let $\mathcal S$ be the
space of smooth flat connections on $\Sigma$ whose class lies in $L(Y)$; a
torus component of $\Sigma$ contributes only the point of $\mathcal M(T)$.
Restriction $\rho:\mathcal P\to\mathcal S$ is a locally trivial fibre bundle,
also over a compact family of primary data. A continuous family
$a:K\times[0,\infty)\to\mathcal S$, smooth in $r$ with all $r$-derivatives
continuous, with a lift $B(\cdot,0)$ of $a(\cdot,0)$, has a lift $B$ with
the same regularity, constant in $r$ where $a$ is.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Near $a_0=\rho(B_0)$ choose a smooth local section $[a]\mapsto\widehat B$ of
$\mathcal P\to L(Y)$ by a primary slice; the primary moduli space is regular
and its restriction map is an embedding (Paper~I, Section~3.2). Then
$\rho(\widehat B)$ and $a$ represent the same class, so $\rho(\widehat B)=g^*a$
for a gauge $g$ near $1$ on $\Sigma$, unique up to the center, by the
surface Coulomb slice and irreducibility on both components. Extend $g$
over the collar by $\exp(\beta\log g)$, with $\beta=1$ on
$[-9/4,-2]\times\Sigma$ and $0$ at depth; this gives a local section with $\rho=a$. Thus $\rho$ is
locally trivial; its fibre is the space of gauge transformations of $Y_0$
which are central constants on the collar, modulo the center. On
$K\times[0,\infty)$ patch the local sections with a partition of unity that
is smooth in $r$ to a connection on the pulled-back bundle; the horizontal
lift solves an ordinary differential equation $g'g^{-1}=\xi$ pointwise on
$Y_0$, so it has the stated regularity and is constant where $a$ is.
\end{proof}

\emph{Positive extension.} For a prepared family, let $\alpha(r,t)$ be the
horizontal lift of $r\mapsto u(r,t)$ in flat connections on $\Sigma$, with
$d^*_{\alpha}\partial_r\alpha=0$ and initial value $b(\theta(t))$; on a torus
component it is constant in $r$, since $H^1(T;\ad)=0$. It exists, is unique
and depends smoothly on $u$, since the flat connections are irreducible;
near $t=\pm1$ it is independent of $t$ by (N2). By
Lemma~\ref{lem:representatives} choose $B(r)$ on $Y_0$ and $B'(r)$ on $-Y_0'$,
products with values $\alpha(r,\pm1)$ on the collars of (N3), with $B(0),B'(0)$
the limit representatives of (N3). Let $A_u$ be the connection on
$[0,\infty)\times Y_\#$ equal to $B(r)$ on $Y_0$, $B'(r)$ on $-Y_0'$, and
$\alpha(r,t)+\widetilde\psi(t)\,dt$ on $[-1,1]\times\Sigma$, with no $dr$
component. By (N3)--(N5) the configuration and $A_u$ are independent of $r$
near $r=0$ and agree there, so the connection $\widetilde A$ on $\RR\times Y_\#$
equal to $A$ for $r\le0$ and to $A_u$ for $r\ge0$ is smooth. For $r\ge S$ it
is independent of $r$; write $c_S$ for its restriction there, whose
$\Sigma$-slices $\alpha(S,t)$ all represent the point determined by $x$.

\begin{lemma}\label{lem:shift}
For $\tau\in[0,S+4]$ put
\[
 A_\tau(r,y)=\widetilde A(r+\tau,y)\ (r\le0),\qquad
 u_\tau(r,t)=u(r+\tau,t).
\]
This is a family of configurations with labels $(a,x)$, jointly continuous
in $(k,\tau)$ and the data, beginning at the prepared family, and it
translates the gauge input end by $\tau$. At $\tau=S+4$ the region
$r\ge-4$ carries $\mathrm{pr}^*c_S$ and $u_\tau\equiv x$. Members of $\mathcal T_x$
remain in $\mathcal T_x$ for all $\tau$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At the seam the slice of $A_\tau$ over $t$ is $\alpha(\tau,t)$, which represents
$u(\tau,t)=u_\tau(0,t)$; the arcs carry $u(r+\tau,\pm1)\in L$. Near a
removed corner the pair is smooth in the reparametrized coordinates, with
limit the stationary pair of $B(\tau)$, whose collar value is $\alpha(\tau,1)$,
a product. In the original end coordinate it converges to this stationary
pair at the exponential rate one. This exceeds the mixed-end weight,
which we choose below one; Proposition~\ref{prop:weights} permits any
weight in the gap. The
configuration therefore lies in the space of Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, with
the mixed limit moving along the arc. Translation over a bounded range is
continuous in that topology. For $\tau=S+4$ and $r\ge-4$ we have $r+\tau\ge S$.
If a member lies in $\mathcal T_x$, then $u\equiv x$ and its seam restriction
is $\mathrm{pr}^*b$, so $\alpha\equiv b$, $\widetilde\psi=0$, $B,B'$ are constant,
and $\widetilde A$ is $\mathrm{pr}^*c$ on $[-3,\infty)\times Y_\#$; the translates
keep this form.
\end{proof}

\emph{Straightening.} At $\tau=S+4$ contract the $t$-dependence of $c_S$. The
slices $\alpha(S,t)$ are independent of $t$ for $|t|\ge1-\epsilon$, by (N2).
Choose a smooth nondecreasing $\rho:[-1,1]\to[-1,1]$ with $\rho(t)=t$ for
$|t|\le1-\epsilon$ and $\rho=\pm1$ near $\pm1$. For $\lambda\in[0,1]$ let
$c_\lambda$ be the connection on $Y_\#$ equal to
$\alpha(S,(1-\lambda)\rho(t))+(1-\lambda)\widetilde\psi(t)\,dt$ on
$[-1,1]\times\Sigma$ and to the lifts $B_\lambda,B'_\lambda$ of
Lemma~\ref{lem:representatives} of the paths
$\lambda\mapsto\alpha(S,\pm(1-\lambda))$, starting at $B(S),B'(S)$. Near
$t=\pm1$ the slices are the constant values of these paths, so the pieces
join smoothly, and at $\lambda=0$ the connection is $c_S$. With a smooth
$\chi$ equal to $0$ for $r\le-5$ and to $1$ for $r\ge-4$, replace the
connection $A_{S+4}$ by
$A_{S+4}+\chi(r)(\mathrm{pr}^*c_\lambda-\mathrm{pr}^*c_S)$ and keep $u\equiv x$. This is
$\mathrm{pr}^*c_\lambda$ on $[-4,0]\times Y_\#$ and the unchanged connection for
$r\le-5$; the interpolation lies in the gauge input end, where no boundary
condition is imposed, and at $\lambda=0$ nothing changes. The slices
represent the point of $x$, so matching holds, and the mixed limits are
stationary pairs of $B_\lambda,B'_\lambda$. At $\lambda=1$ the connection
$c_1=B_1\cup\mathrm{pr}^*b\cup B'_1$ is independent of $t$ on the neck, with
$B_1,B'_1$ products on the collars of (N3). On members of $\mathcal T_x$ the
slices $\alpha(S,\cdot)$ are constant and $\widetilde\psi=0$, so this step is
the identity.

\emph{Collar widening.} Let $K_1$ be the temporal gauge of $B_1\cup\mathrm{pr}^*b$
along the flat collar, as in the proof of Lemma~\ref{lem:preparation}; it
equals $1$ on $[-9/4,0]$. Choose a smooth $\sigma_W:[-3,0]\to[-3,0]$ with
$\sigma_W(s)=s$ on $[-5/2,0]$ and $\sigma_W=0$ near $-3$, put
$g_\mu=K_1(\mu\sigma_W(s))$ on $Y_0$, extended by $1$, and define $g'_\mu$ on
$Y_0'$ likewise. For $\mu\in[0,1]$ these gauge transformations equal $1$ on
$[-9/4,0]\times\Sigma$, hence near the neck and the seam, and
$g_1^*B_1$ is a product on $[-5/2,-2]\times\Sigma$. Apply the gauge
transformation $g_{\mu\chi(r)}\cup1\cup g'_{\mu\chi(r)}$ on the gauge region,
with $\chi$ as above. It changes the mixed limits to the stationary pairs
of $g_\mu^*B_1$ and $g'^*_\mu B'_1$, and at $\mu=1$ the region $r\ge-4$
carries $\mathrm{pr}^*c$ with $c=g_1^*B_1\cup\mathrm{pr}^*b\cup g'^*_1B'_1$, a
representative of the critical point over $x$ in the sense above. The
configuration therefore lies in $\mathcal T_x$. A member of $\mathcal T_x$
is carried to the constant pair of $g_\mu^*B\cup\mathrm{pr}^*b\cup g'^*_\mu B'$,
which is again a representative since $g_\mu=1$ on $[-5/2,0]$ in that case;
so members of $\mathcal T_x$ remain in $\mathcal T_x$.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(a) for finite torus lengths]
Concatenate Lemma~\ref{lem:preparation}, Lemma~\ref{lem:shift} and the
straightening, and transport by the identifications of
Section~\ref{app:shift-reduction}. Relative preparation of the original
tails is the second step of Lemma~\ref{lem:preparation}. Continuity in the
data holds because every choice above (the collar gauges, the charts at
stationary pairs, the lifts of Lemma~\ref{lem:representatives}, the horizontal
lifts, the widening gauges) depends continuously on the primary data, and
the identifications depend continuously on the lengths. The deformation is
the identity on, or a translation of, the gauge input end away from
$[-5,-3]\times Y_\#$. Hence if
the family has the form $A_0\#_\ell c$ with an ordinary configuration $A_0$
glued at the input with a long neck $\ell$, the deformation is
$A_0\#_{\ell+\tau}c_\tau$, and the determinant gluing maps of
Lemma~\ref{lem:line-gluing} are continuous along it. This is the coherence with
ordinary end gluing. Families with strips glued at the output are families of
the same kind.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Completed torus domains}\label{app:shift-completed}
Let a compact family on $W_{a,\infty}$ be given. By
Lemma~\ref{lem:split-preglue}(i) it is in torus normal form beyond some
$r_0$ after a continuous family of small gauge transformations. The
truncation $\alpha+(1-t+t\chi_{R})b$ on the tail, with $\chi_R=1$ for
$r\le R$ and $0$ for $r\ge R+1$, is continuous in the topology of
Section~\ref{sec:geometry}, whose weight is independent of $r$, and fixes
members that are $\alpha$ beyond $R$; at $t=1$ every member is $\alpha$ beyond
$R+1$. Such a configuration is the extension by $\alpha$ of a configuration on
$W_{a,R+2}$ which equals $\alpha$ near the flat face, and the face is part of
the matching seam. The deformations of
Sections~\ref{app:shift-prep}--\ref{app:shift-shift} preserve this condition:
the collar gauges and the chart cutoffs are the identity where the
configuration and its limit are $\alpha$, the widening gauges are supported
away from the neck and the seam, the collar pullbacks fix
configurations equal to $\alpha$ near the seam, the seam term $\widetilde\psi$
vanishes on the torus component, the horizontal lifts and the representatives
are $\alpha$ on $T$, and the translation and the straightening bring the
positive extension, which is $\alpha$ on $T$, to the neighbourhood of the face.
Extending by $\alpha$ along the whole deformation gives
Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(a) on $W_{a,\infty}$, ending in $\mathcal T_x$
with a representative which is $\alpha$ on $T$; the constant pair is then $\alpha$
on the tail.

\subsection{The localized several-input shift}\label{app:shift-local}
On a triangle or folded triangle at positive collapse scale, and on a
finite-width joined torus domain, the outgoing closed cut writes the gauge
region as an ordinary cobordism followed by an output region $M$. For the
joined torus domain this is Paper~I, Lemma~10.18; for the triangles and
folded triangles it is the cut of the proofs of Paper~I, Theorems~9.10
and~11.8, through the collapsing region (Paper~I, Lemmas~9.11 and~9.12).
In each case $M$, with a smooth product collar at the cut, is identified
with the complement of $(-\infty,-c_1]\times Y_\#$ in a special quintuple by a
diffeomorphism of decorated manifolds, continuous in the width and in the
scale on compact positive ranges; its compression bodies are those of the
pair $(D_0,D_1)$, or of the marked pair of the triangle. The cut is
$\{-c_1\}\times Y_\#$, with $c_1>5$. The steps of
Sections~\ref{app:shift-prep}--\ref{app:shift-shift} are supported in
$r\ge-5$, away from a collar of the cut, except for the translation. Replace
the translation by the localized map: with $\chi$ nondecreasing, zero near
$-c_1$ and one on $[-5,\infty)$, put $F_\kappa(r)=r+\kappa\chi(r)$ and
\[
 A_\kappa=F_\kappa^*\widetilde A,\qquad u_\kappa(r,t)=u(r+\kappa,t).
\]
Since $F_\kappa'>0$ this is a smooth configuration; it is a translation on
$[-5,\infty)$, so the seam and the corners are treated as in
Lemma~\ref{lem:shift}, and near the cut it is the identity, so it glues with
the ordinary cobordism. For $\kappa=S+4$ followed by the straightening and
the collar widening, the region $r\ge-4$ carries a constant pair $C^c$, and the configuration is
$A_E\#C^c$, with $A_E$ an ordinary cobordism configuration ending at $c$ on
the parallel copy $\{-7/2\}\times Y_\#$ of the cut, whose collar
$[-4,-3]\times Y_\#$ carries $\mathrm{pr}^*c$, and with the original inputs
unchanged. This is the deformation of Section~\ref{sec:determinants}, which
proves Proposition~\ref{prop:shifting}(b). Relative and continuity statements
follow as in Lemma~\ref{lem:shift}.

\section{A sign calculation for projective composition}\label{app:signs}

The conversion of a strictly
commuting map $F$ of degree
$f$ to a closed map is
$F^{\mathrm r}=F\eps^f$.
For another strictly
commuting map $G$ of degree
$g$, the relation
$\eps^fG=(-1)^{fg}G\eps^f$
gives
\[
 F^{\mathrm r}G^{\mathrm r}
   =(-1)^{fg}FG\eps^{f+g}
   =(-1)^{fg}(FG)^{\mathrm r}.
\]
Thus this convention change
is compatible with
composition after
projectivization. The
factor is scalar, despite
the grading operator used
in converting each map.

For a strict homotopy
$F-F'=dH+Hd$, where
$|H|=f+1$, the correctly
converted homotopy is
$H\eps^f$. Indeed
\[
 \delta(H\eps^f)
 =dH\eps^f-(-1)^{f+1}H\eps^fd
 =(dH+Hd)\eps^f.
\]
Using $H\eps^{f+1}$ instead
would give the wrong sign.
This calculation is useful
whenever a gauge
composition homotopy is
translated into the Hom
complex convention.

Lastly, if
$F_i-G_i=\delta K_i$ and
the maps are closed,
a homotopy between two
composites is obtained
by replacing one factor
at a time and using
\eqref{eq:compose-homotopy}.
For example, for three
factors one may take
\[
 K_3F_2F_1
 +(-1)^{|G_3|}G_3K_2F_1
 +(-1)^{|G_3|+|G_2|}G_3G_2K_1.
\]
Applying $\delta$ telescopes
to $F_3F_2F_1-G_3G_2G_1$.
This is the chain-level
algebra used in
Lemma~\ref{lem:descent}.
